THE A.P.ASSIGNED LANDS (PROHIBITION OF TRANSFERS) ACT,1977
(ACT No.9/1977)
1) Application:- The Act extends to the whole of the state of Andhra Pradesh
2) Enforcement:- The Act came into force on the 21" January 1977

3) Objects: The Act is intended to prohibit alienation of lands assigned to landless poor persons
cD’ form Pattas) and provide for resumption of such Jands alienated and to regrant to eligible
persons.

4) Definitions:

a) “Assigned Land” means lands assigned by the State Government to the Landless poor
persons subject to the condition of non-alienation by the grantees and includes land allotted
or transferred to landless poor persons under the relevant law for the time being in force
relating to land ceilings and the word “assigned” shall beconstructed accordingly (Section
2(1)).

Explanation : Mortgage in favour of the following shall not be regarded as an alienation
Viz., ~

i) Central or State Government or any loca! authority
 

 

{
:
f
|
:
:
f

205
iil) Any registered Co-operative Society i bee 6 . hl. E
ii) Any Bank including Agricultural Development Bank, 7” Deal _

iv) Reserve Bank of India

    

v) “State Bank of India and its subsidiary Banks b

vi) Corresponding new Bank Constituted under Section 3 of the Banking companies
(Acquisition and Transfer of undertaking Act 1970(Central Act 5 of 1970)):

b) “Landless Poor Person” means a person who owns land not moré than 1.011715 Hectares
{two and half acres) of wet land or 2.023430. hectares (five acres) of dry land and such

‘other extent of land as has been or may be specified by: the state government in this behalf
from time to time and who has no other means of livelihood (Section 2(3)).

c) “Transfer” means any sale, gift, exchange, mortgage with or without possession, lease or
any other transactions with assigned lands, not being a testamentary disposition and includes
a charge on such property or a contract relating to assigned lands in respect of such sale,
gift, exchange, mortgage, lease or other transactions.

5) Prohibition of transfer of assigned land:-

Any person to. whom any land has been assigned shall not transfer such land and should be
deemed never to have been transferred and accordin ely ho tight or title to’such assigned land will
vest in any person acquiring the land by-such transfer (Section 3).

6) Prohibition of Registration of ‘Assigned Lands:
NOT WITHSTANDING ANYTHING CONTAINED:IN THE REGISTRATION ACT,
1908, Registering Officer shall not accept for registration, any document relating to the transfer of

or the creation of any interest in any assigned land included in a list of assigned lands except under
prior permission of the District Collector. The District Collector shall furnish a list of assigned lands

in the district to the registering officers concemed (Section'5)

for confirmation.

    

7) Act to override other laws:- The provisions of the A.P. Assigned Lands (POT) Act 1977
shalf have effect‘not withstanding ‘anything inconsistent therewith contained in any other law for
the time being in force or any custom, usage, or contract or decree or order of a court., tribunal or
other Authority:

Note:- The Sub Registrars, in memo No.G1/32938/92 Dated 20-01-1993 of the Inspector
General of Registration:and Stamps. were’ instructed to.obtain upto —date list of assigned lands

 

 
206

from the Mandal Revenue officers and to ensure that no document affecting assigned land is

registered on pain of drastic action against Sub Registrars who contravene the provisions of the
Act.

8) Rules : - The District Collector or the officer authorized by the District Collector shall as soon
as an order is passed assigning land to a land less poor person, furnish a copy of the order to the
Registration department so as to prevent the department from registering any document relating to
the transfer of such land (Rule 4 of the A.P. Assigned Lands (POT) Rules 1977).

9) Land assigned to Ex-Service men:- The land assigned to the Ex-service men can be alienated
on expiry of ten years after assignment (Vide G.O.Ms.No. 1117 Revenue Dept. Dated 11-11-1993)

10) The transfer of any land assigned by the Government to a Landless poor person for cultivation
or as a house site is prohibited under the above said Act. Therefore, the registering officer shal!
satisfy himself that the property involved in a document presented for registration is not an assigned
land and also by duly incorporating the fact in the document by the transferor (S.0.459)

APPENDIX - LA
REGISTRATION RULES (ACT 16 of 1999)
(as amended by G.O.Ms.No.s722 Rev (Regn. 1) dt 8-10-199)
CHAPTER XXXII
REGISTRATION OF DOCUMENTS THROUGH CARD

221, Definitions: In this Chapter, unless the context otherwise required, the following words and
expressions shall have the meaning assigned to them, namely:-

(i) ‘Archival’ means capturing data, including images, on to electronic storage media like the
C.D., the tape, the hard-disk and the like, with the intention of preserving the same for long
periods and for retrieving when tequired and includes re-archival.

Gi} *‘CARD" or ‘Computer-aided Administration of Registration Department’ means the process
of performing the various functions associated with the act of registration, through electronic
devices like computers and scanners, to ensure an efficient, accurate and transparent
delivery of services top the registering public.

(ii). ‘CD or Compact Disk’ means an electronic storage device on which data, including images,
can be stored in an electronic digital form.

(Gv) ‘CD Writer’ means an electronic device used to copy data available in digital form on an
electronic storage device on to a CD.

(v) “Hard ware’ includes the electronic devices like computers, scanners, printers, CD writers
which aré captured, store and process data in digital form.
oar

 

(vi) ‘Imaging’ means thé’ process ‘of scinsing the documents”and managing the storage,
~~ classification and retrieval of the eleciiGnic digital images-8o generated.

(vii) ‘Scanner’ means an electronic device used in conjunction with a computer and suitable
software, to convert documents on paper into cléctronic digital images to be stored on:
electronic media‘and retrieved when’ required, 3 andthe words “scannitg’ ‘and ‘scanned’
shail be construed accordingly. .

    

(viii) ‘Software’ includes a set of computer porgrammes or coded oinsiructions g nm to the
computer systems to make the latter perform different, predetermined functions and generate
the desired output. ‘ si

222. Responsibility of the Inspector ‘General:

. The Inspector General of Registration shall be responsible for administration of CARD
and for ensuring: substantial compliance with the provisions of this chapter. For this purpose.

«the Inspector Genéral may issue suitable circular instructions and such i instructions I have
the force of standing order for strict compliance by all the registering officers.

2. For the purposes of the sub-rule(1), the Inspector General shall specify, from time to time,
the configuration of hardware and the software to be used in different categories of
registration offices as may be required. : .

3. The Inspector General shall also cause supply of the hardware and the software specified
in sub-rule(2) at all the registering offices, i in respect of which notification has been issued
by the Government under section 70B of the Act. .

4. It shall be basic responsibility of the registering officers to enstire that the ‘hardware and

~~ software so supplied is always kept in a good state to ensure the continued availability of

‘the CARD services. The registering officers shall, at all times, use software notified and
certified by the Inspector General and no ‘other software.”

__5. The Inspector General shiall make adequate’ arrangemeiits, including’ niainténance of the
“required stand by systems and spares and retention of the servicés' of technical personnel,
as may requiréd to enable the registering officers to comply with sub-rule(4).

Rule 223 to 237 contain the detailed procedure for enforcement of the provision’ of the system
under CARD project as detailed hereunder. .,

 

223. Procedure to be followed: The registering officers shall follow the provisions of the Act
and rules in all matters other than those covered by this chapter. Ini casé of'a conflict between any
provision of this chapter and.any other rule, the provision of-this chapter s shall prevail in relation to
any compertized process specified in this chapter.

224, Presentation of Documents:

(2) “When a document is presented to him, the registering officer shall satisfy hitnself thatitis fitto-
be accepted for registration with reference'to all the provisions of the“Act; rules and‘standing

 
208

orders. In addition, he shall satisfy himself that the writing is legible and not faint or indistinct
and the documents is written and signed in dark black ink such that it is fit for being scanned
property.

(2) All documents presented for registration under Computer Aided Administration of Registration
Department, shall be accompanied by an input form in the proforma given in Appendix XI. The
registering officer may arrange to provide such assistance to the registering public as may be
required to enable them to fill up the input forms and to avail the various services offered by
the department without any difficulty.

(3) Upon satisfying himself that the provisions of the sub-rules(1) and(2) are complied with, the
registering officer shall affix his signature on the input form indicating the date and time of
presentation.

(4) The registering officer shall thereupon cause the presentation endorsement to be impressed in
black ink or to be printed by the Computer Aided Administration of Registration Department
system as specified in rule 52(1) (a) on the reverse of the first stamp paper used for writing the
document.

(5) The procedure required under section 35 of the Act shall then be followed with regard to the
admission of execution.

(6) The registering officer shall then send the document together with the input form to the computer
section for further process under the CARD system.

225. Registering Check Slip:

(1) The details of.the executants, claimants, nature of the document, description of the property
together with its boundaries shall be entered in the computer and a check slip printed and
handed over to the person presenting the document under an acknowledgement. The check
slip shall be in the format shown in the Appendix XT.

(2) The mistakes in spelling or in the description of the property or its boundaries, pointed by the
party, in writing and under signature after verification of the check slip, shall be corrected in
the computer and a revised check slip shall be printed and handed over to the party.

226. Issue of Receipt:

(1) The deficit stamp duty, if any, and the registration fee and other amounts indicated in the
registration check slip, shall then be collected and a receipt printed by the computer shall be
issued to the party. The receipt shall be in format shown in the Appendix XIIL

(2) On payment of the deficit stamp duty, registration fee and other amounts, the document together

with input form, the registration check slip and the receipt shall be sent to the registering
officer for verification. ~

 

ee
227. Verification by the registering officer: The details of the registration check slip and the
receipt shall be verified by the registering officer with reference to the original document to satisfy

 
 

 

i
i
f

209

himself as to the compliance with the Act, rules and the standing orders and the adequacy of the
stamp duty paid.

228. Registration of the documents:

(1) After completion of the procedure prescribed in the rules 223 to 227, the registering officer
shall admit the document to registration in terms of the provisions of the section 58 and 59.

(2) The registering officer shall then assign a regular number to the document and mark in the
dark ink on all the sheets of the documents.

229. Endorsement and certificates:

(1) The endorsements and certificates. required. to be. made under: section. 58.and..59 and the
relevant Registration Rules, shall be made on the document in accordance with the procedure
prescribed.

(2) The certificate of registration required to be made under'section 60 of the Act;shall‘be made
in the format shown below in respect of the documents registered under the Computer aided
Administration of Registration Department system?

Registered No............ Of Lee of Book To... day. of, 200-1. suterteeee S.E

: Signature of) Registering officer.
(3) The endorsements may be made by using any 01 or a combination of the following methods.
(i) By. writing in hand using black ink.
Gi) By iniptessing a rubber stamp using black ink.

Gili) By getting the endorsements and certificates printed ‘suitabl ly on the reverse of the
stamp papers using the feature provided for the purpose in the Computer-aided
Administration of Registration Department system.

(4) The endorsement and certificates, however so made as above shall be, authenticated by the
registering officer.

230. Scanning of the documents:

(1) After the process of affixing the endorsements and ¢ertificates in completed, the document
shall be scanned, on both the sides of all the sheeted including the maps and plans accompanying
the document, using the scanner and the imaging software provided.

(2) The registering officer shall satisfy himself that the document has been properly scanned
following the procedure laid down by the Inspector:General in this’ behalf:-Thereupon, the
following certificated shall be affixed on the reverse of the stamp paper used for writing the
documents, below the certificate of registration. , :

 
210
Certificate of Scanning

The document has been scanned with the Idei‘ification Number............6064

Signature of the Registering officer.

(3) The documents presented for registration and registered alone should be scanned and copics
of the document should not be scanned.

231. Return of the Documents:

(1) After satisfying himself that the procedures prescribed in the rules 223 to 230 are com plied
with and-especially that the document has been properly scanned the registering officer shall
retum the document to the person authorized to receive the same, duly obtaining an
acknowledgement therefor.

 

(2) For the purpose of monitoring and recording the ~ceipt and return of the document, the registering
officer shall maintain a “Document Register’ in vie format shown in the Appendix XIV, Entries
in the columns (1) to (8) shall be made at the time of presentation, in ike columns(9) while
assigning a regular number to the document and in columns (10) to (14) while returning the
document to the authorized person.

232. Archiving of the images:

(1) The images of the scanned documents together with the data relating thereto shail be archived
on the CD’s or tapes suitably labeled, using the CD writer and the computers under the
Computer-aided Administration of Registration Department system, as soon as the documents
are scanned.

(2) CD or tape after it is completely filled with images of scanned documents, duplicate and
triplicate copies of such a CD ot tape shall be generated following such procedure as may be
specified by the Inspector General in this behalf.

(3) The Duplicate copy of CD or tape shall be suitably labeled and sealed and shail be sent to the
District Registrar within three days from the date of generation.

(4) The Duplicate copies of CDs or Tapes shall be preserved with the District Registrar and shall
be used for being produced as evidence whenever summoned by Courts. The Triplicate copies
of CDs and Tapes shall be sent to Inspector General of Registration and Stamps in the first
week of January, April July and October every year for preservation.

(5) The CDs and the tapes shall be preserved in such conditions and taking such precautions as
may be specified by the Inspector General in this behalf.

(6) The CDs and tapes shall be recopied or re-archived at such periodic intervals as the Inspector
General may specify.

233. Documents registered manually:-

(1) Certain documents will have to be registered manually under the following circumstances.

236. Revocation, cancellation and rectification of deeds already, registered:.

(1) When a deed purporting to revoke, cancel or rectify a ‘deed previously-registered under the
manual! system, is presented for registration, such deed may be registered following the
procedure prescribed in this Chapter and the foot notes specified under rate 1 18, shall be made

(2) When a deed purporting to revoke, cancel or rectify adeed previously tegistered under the

such deed may be registered following the procedure prescribed in this chapter and contra
entries posted to the record relating to the original deed and a memo in the nature of a foot note
shall be appended to such record so that the foot note in printed invariably when the original
document is sought to be printed.

237. Security:

(1) Adequate security systems shall be developed and implemented to ensure that the data and
images of the documents registered under the Computer-aided Administration of Registration
Department system are preserved without any scope for loss, corruption or unauthorised access

“devices.

(2.

It shall be the responsibility of registering officer and all the employees authorised to handle the
systems to ensure that the security measures prescribed are strictly adhered to and that the
passwords and access devices are maintained confidentially at all times.

(3) The Inspector General shall review the security plan periodically, at feast once a year, to
ensure that the security standards of the highest order are always maintained.

APPENDIX 1-B (The Position as on 22-1-1999)

The commissioner and Inspector General of Registration and stamps in his circular
No.38 dated 22-1-1999, has issued instructions for governing the process of registration under
the card project. An extract of the circular is reproduced hereunder for ready reference.

Registration process under the CARD system:

(a) Presentation: (1) The document presented to the Registering officer shall be scrutinized
by the registering officer w.rt classification and the appropriate transaction code
assigned/ticked on the input form, initialed and passed on to the computer counter, if
the instrument related to sale, gift(including gift settlement) or mortgage with all the
other variants of-these 3 classes of instruments. It may be noted that only these 3
classes of instruments have been notified by the Government as of now. All other
documents shall be handled manually till further orders. (Further orders are since issued
in respect of other documents)

(2) Presentation at private residence: The existing procedure may be followed in
respect of documents necessitating attendance at a private residence, to the extent of
presentation and admission of execution only, Further processing like generation of
registration checkslip and scanning shall be done on computer.

5. Copying, Indexing And Accounting: CARD system seeks to eliminate a lot of unproduc-
tive work and thereby enhance speed and efficiency of registration, The following revised
procedures are prescribed in this-regard.

Copying: Copying of document; endorsements certificates shall be dispensed with on and
from 5-2-99, It should, however, be ensured that the document is properly scanned before it is
returned to he party, As a measure of additional precaution, a laser print of the document should be
taken immediately after scanning and filed in the volume in Category A and B offices. This proce-
dure of taking laser prints shall be continued upto 31-3-99 and dispensed with from 1-4-99. In
Category C offices, the documents scanned should be copied on the TAPE and print outs taken
once a week on the laser printer available with the nearest Cat B or A office and filed.

Archiving: The documents scanned should be archived at the end of each day on to the CD (in A
& B offices). or to the tape in Cat.C officers. A fresh CD/tape shall be started on 5-2-99 for this
purpose. Once a CD/tape is full, as would be indicated by the scanning client software automati-
cally, the session should be closed and a duplicate copy of the CD/Tape generated immediately.
Once this is done, the system would request permission to delete the images of documents scanned
and the same should be done after testing the CD’s/Tapes in duplicate. One copy of the CD/tape
shall then be sent to the DR under acknowledgement and the other copy preserved safely with the
SR. .

Indexing: Both index I & II should be dispensed with from 5-2-99. The CARD system automati-
cally maintains these indexes.

Accounting: The accounts in respect of all documents should be continued manually till 31-3-99.
Separate instructions will be issued with regard to dispensing with the accounts from 1-1-99.

6. Documents Registered Manually: Certain documents will have to be registered manually.
for the following reasons:

(a) Documents other than sale, mortgage and gift are since notified to be registered under the
CARD system.

(b) The CARD system may be out of order at times, In such case, even the documents
relating to sale, gift and mortgage will have to be registered using the manual system,
However, the registering officer should make a specific endorsement in the minutes book,

specifying the reasons necessitating the resort to manual system.

 

 

 

 
 

 

215

(c) The details of documents registered manually for:the reason b) above shall be entered into

the computers, following instructions that will be separately issued, soon after th the CARD
“system, is restored: °

_.. PART-2
STAMP ACT

The Stamp’ Act is purcly a’fiscal statuite dealing with tax on transactions carried out through
instruments. It’s sole object is to'inierease’and’ protect State Revenues

The Registration and Stamps Department plays a large part in safeguarding the stamp
revenue and plug leakages. A majority:of the documents chargeable with duty require registration
compulsorily under the Indian Registration, Act, 1908. So, such documents come into the hands of
the Registering officers. They ensure that proper stamp duty is paid on ‘them. If not, the documents
are impounded and adjudicated and revenue lost together with penalties are levied and collected.

The Stamp Act taxes the instruments but not the transactions. But it is the substance of the
transaction as evidenced in the instrument that determines the duty but not the force of the document.
Tt is not the form or title given to the document that should be taken into account but the transaction
as embodied in the document.

The Registering officers need. not be blindly carried away, by! the recitals in the document.
He can ascertain further facts’ when he has reason to believe that the vital facts and. circumstances
affecting the changeability of an instrument to duty were not fully and truly brought out in the
document. £

Stamp duty is atax,but not fee,for:services rendered. Inthe Act, provision is made
empowering State Government to grant exemptions from payment of stamp duty prospectively or
retrospectively, wholly or partially, in respect Of any instrument or pafticular class of instruments or
any of the instruments belonging to such class or any instruments when éxecuted by or in favour of
any particular class of persons or by or in favour of any member of such class are chargeable and
also for the composition or consolidation of duties in the case of issues by any incorporated company
or other body corporate or of transfer of debentures, bonds or other marketable securities.

The Actalso hag made provision for validation of instruments not duly stamped, instruments
executed outside Andhra Pradesh in Indian‘ and abroad and for supply and sale of stamps of
various categories to the public.

The Act contains two schedules —-Schedule I and Schedule I-A prescribing rates of stamp
duty payable on various kinds of documents in the purview of Central Government (Schedule 1)
and those in the purview of the ‘State Government (Schedule I-A).

THE INDIAN STAMP: ACT, 1899
CHAPTER - i

The provisions of the Indian Stamp Act 1899 and the Indian Stamp Rules, 1925, sectionwise
and rulewise respectively, in its application in respect of instruments specified in the Schedules J
and I-A to the Stamp Act are briefly stated below.

Section. 1: The Section stipulates that the Act extends to the whole of India except the State of
Jammu and Kashmir

Section. 2: The Section provides among others, the meanings of the following words, expres-
sions and terms used in the Stamp Act (Vide Chapter 2).

1. Bill of Exchange (Sec. 2(2)) 10. Impressed stamp (Sec.2(13))

2. Bill of Exchange payable on demand (Sec.2(3)) _—‘L1._Instrument (Sec.2(14))

3. Bond (Sec.2(5)) 12. Instrument of Partition (Sec.2(15))
4.. Chargeable (Sec.2(6)) . 13, Lease (Sec.2(16))

5. CCRA (Since repealed) (Sec.2(7)) 14. Mortgage deed (Sec.2(17))

6. Collector (Sec.2(9)) 15. Power of attorney (Sec.2(21))

7. Conveyance (Sec.2(10)) 16. Promissory note (Sec.2(22))

8.) Duly stamped (Sec.2(1!)) ; 17. Receipt (Sec.2(23))

9... Executed & Execution (Sec.2(12)) 18. Settlement (Sec.2(24))

The interpretations of the terms contained in this Section are useful only for the purpose of this
Act. i.e., for ascertaining the amount of stamp duty with which an instrument is chargeable and
great caution must be exercised in applying them for other purposes.

Section. 3: (1) This Section is the charging Section. It states what.documents are liable to duties
under the Act. Such documents are listed out in Schedule J and Schedule J-A to the Act. Docu-
ments not mentioned in: the Schedules are not liable to duty. There are exemptions from duty in
various cases as provided for in the body of the Act or in Schedule I or I-A. The thing which is
made liable to duty is the instrument but not the transaction. Liability to stamp duty arises only on
the execution. ,

(2) It is the substance. of the transaction as embodied in the instrument and not the form of the
instrument that determines the duty. It is not the name that is given to the instrument that
determines the duty. A document may be styled as a Release but it may amount to a Convey-
ance. Regard should be paid to the substance (but not the form) of the transaction as embodied
in the instrument while determining the duty.

(3) However sections 5 and 6 make a difference from this principle. They take the transaction into
consideration in determining the duty but not the instrument
232

(4): Proviso (1) under subsectiori 3(b)(b) ‘stipulates that in the: case of : any instrament exe
» of of behalf of orin favout'of the Government, where the. vernment would be liable, to pay:
the duty in terms of Sec. 29, such an instrument is “exempted from payment of stai
chargeable in respect of such instrument: Bg: Coniveyarice, Lease étc.

~ chargeable with the scribed duty ‘under Schedule 1 or 1A. as the case. may be and that each
other instrument shall be chargeable with a duty of Rs.5/- only

   

 

 

Section. 5: The Section provides. that an instrument comprising several distinct :mattersto be
charged with aggregate : amount of duties with which: separate instrument in'respect of each distinct
matter would have. been chargeable. Distinct.matter,connots distinct transaction. If the transac-
tions are distinct and separate, but happen to be embodied in one document, that document must ‘be
treated as comprising several distinct matters. The following are a few examples which may be
viewed as falling under Section 5 chargeable with the ageregate amount of the duties:

 

 

 

iti, Power of attorney execuited i in favour of a person. for, several purposes;

 

 

iv. Sale for Rs. 5000/- with an-agreerient-to resell (sale and‘agreement) ©

Section. 6: The Section applies to instruments so framed as to come within two.or more of the
descriptions in Schedule I or Schedule I-A. Where the duties chargeable thereunder are different,
be chargeable with the highest of such duties.

The following are a few examples which may be viewed as falling under section’ 6,

i. In the case of duplicates or counterparts (Art 22 of Sch. J-A) of any instrument chargeable
with duty‘and the proper duty has been paid thereon the proviso under the section regulate
the duties payable on such duplicate or counterpart restricting to payment of a Stamp duty
of Rupees Five only on such a counterpart or a duplicate.

ii, By an indenture executed by A and B, A leased his immovable property to B for 10 years
and B gave his. immovable property as security for two years rent if it fell in arrears.

iii, A, the guardian of C sold the properties of the minor for Rs.20000/- to B. In the same
document he hypothecated his own properties worth Rs. 1 Lakh indemnifying to the
extent of Rs.50000/-. The document is a “Sale with indemnity”.

iv, A sold his property to C for Rs.5000/-. B, to whom the property was previously mort-
gaged joined the transaction and expressly released his mortgage right over the property,
The document is a sale and release of mortgage right. (M LJ commentary on Section 5).

v. Sale with indemnity
vi. Lease with indemnity

vii. Agreement to carry mails for an annual consideration of Rs.6000/- and.a security deposit
of Rs.1000/-. The document is chargeable only as an Agreement under Section 6. Cash
deposit towards security does not attract stamp duty.

viti_A hypothecated certain immovable property to B for Rs.5000/-.and agreed to perform
agricultural labour in lieu of interest. A gave a lease of his land to B for an annual rent of
Rs.500/- for 10years, B, in turn hypothecated his land to secure payment of yearly rent.

ix. A and B running a partnership concern dissolved the same and divided the assets of
Rs.100000/- in two shares. (Dissolution and Partition)

x. A settled on his sons B,C & D separate items of properties worth Rs.10000/-, 15000/-
and 18000/-.An exchange deed whereby properties worth Rs.10000/- each were ex-
changed between A and B. As there was some dispute regarding the property owned by
B, C joined as party and gives indemnity of the property for Rs. 10000/- to A.(Settlement
and indemnity)

xi. Divorce deed entered into between A and B, husband and wife, dissolving their marriage.
The wife also relinquished her right for future maintenance for a consideration of
Rs. L000/- (Divorce and release). -

xii. Chit agreement executed by two or more subscribers in favour of the Foreman.

Section.13: The Section requires the stamp paper to be so written on that the stamp may appear
on the face of the Instrument.

There is no prohibition for the writing of the document on both sides of the stamp paper
though it is desirable to write only on the face of the stamp paper alone (Note 2 under 13 and Note
2 under Rule 7(2)).

Section.14: The Section prohibits writing of another instrument on the same stamp paper used for
an instrument chargeable. to duty and for which the writing of that instrument has already been
completed.

If a material alteration is made in an executed document, it amounts to writing a second
document (Sec: 14 read with section 15 and Note 7 under Section 14 A.P.S.M).

In a document presented for registration, if the name of the claimant is.found struck off
and another name substituted, the time of alteration should be ascertained. If the alteration was
made after execution, the document should be deemed as unstamped , impounded and submitted to
the Distinct Registrar who will levy the deficit duty and may remit the penalty (Proviso to Section

40(i)(0)).

A material alternation is one, which were made after execution of the document and
varies the rights, liabilities or legal position of the parties ascertained by the deed in its original state
or otherwise varies the legal effect of the instrument as originally expressed

The following may be treated as material alternations if such alterations are © made after
execution of the document.

Alteration of time of payment

Alteration of date of payment

Alteration of sum payable

Alteration of interest payable

Alteration in statement of consideration.

Alteration in name of executants or claimants
+ Alteration of number of Bank rate

Section.15: The section is a natural corollary to the sections 13 and 14 - It provides that an

instrument written in contravention of Section 13 and 14 shall be deemed to bé unstamped with its
consequences :
236

Section.16: The section provides for “denoting duty” paid in respect of another instrument by way
of an endorsement on the instrument the chargeability of which depends in any manner upon the
duty paid in respect, of another instrument.

The object of this section isto save the parties from the trouble‘of producing the second
instrument by providing that the payment of duty on the second instrument may be denoted on the
first instrument-by endorsement under. the-hand of the Collector. For this purpose, this section
requires that an application in writing with proper court fee must be made to the Collector and that
both the instruments must be produced before him. The section doesnot make it obligatory 6n the
party, to make an application. But if such an application is made, Collector must act under this
section.

In the following cases, the'duty payable on an instrument depends upon the duty paid on
other instrument: © : - . °

(i) Subsidiary instruments chargeable under Section 4.
(i) Counterpart or duplicate chargeable under Article 22... .

(ii) Leases under Article 31, deeds ofpartition under Article 40_and.settlements
under Article 49, where duty has been paid on antecedent agreements.

As per §.0.476 (c).() a certificate under Sec. 16 is required in respect of (1) Counterparts
(2) Duplicates (3) Supplemental Deeds(4) Sales in favour of Mortgages stamped under the last
proviso to Sec.24 of the Stamp Act(5) Further charge with possession on Simple mortgage(6)
Auxiliary, Collateral, Additional and Substituted security (7) Lease, partition and settlement stamped
under Articles 31, 40.and.49, respectively of Schedule I-A and (8) Lease deed executed in favour
of Mortgagee without possession falling under.the explanation to Article 35 of Schedule LA. (9)
Sale deed in pursuance of an agreement to sell with possession stamped under Art.47(A) of Schedule
TA.

This certificate is unnecessary in respect of. transfers of Mortgage.
(a) > The certificate on item (4) above; shall be in the following form.

“I hereby certify that on the production of Mortgage Deed executed in favour of the
Vendee or of the person from whom the Vendee has obtained ‘the Mortgage rights in
respect of property .dealtwith herein I have: satisfied myself that the stamp duty of
Rs._____ has been paid therefor”

(b) ° The certificate in respect of item 8 shall be the same as-above with the word
“Lessee” substituted in the place of “Vendee” wherever that word occurs.

(c) "The certificate on documents other than those mentioned in items 4& 8 shall be
in the following form,

“T hereby certify that on the production of the original instrument, I have satisfied myself
that the stamp duty of Rs. . _has been paid thereof.”
237

(a) Copies of original documents or their counterpart can be admitted in evidence
under Sec. 16 of Stamp Act only in rare and exceptional cases, where the party
declares that the original documents are either lost or cannot be produced. In stich
cases, the form of Certificate prescribed above shall be altered suitably.

(e)  Anapplication under Sec. 16 of the Stamp Act is to be submitted duly affixed with
a C.F label of Re. 1/- G's Procgs. No. G1/25493/64, dt. 25-2-1964)

Section.17: This section applies to instruments executed in India. It makes provision as regards
the time of stamping of instruments executed in India.

Stamp duty should be paid before or at the time of execution. Affixing of stamp after
execution is invalid (vide Note 4 under Sec,.13.) Sec. LOA however permits payment of the duty by
cash as prescribed therein when there is shortage of stamps in any district or stamps of required
denominations are not available. Vide Chapter 16 for Guide Lines.

A portion of the document should be written on each sheet. The signature of parties and
attesting witnesses do not form part of the document (Rule 7(1) and Note 2 thereunder and Sec.,13).

Tt is tregular to attach blank stamp paper to make up the deficiency in stamp duty after
execution of the document (Sec. 17)

Section.18: The section applies to instruments other than Bills and Promissory Notes executed
out of India. When a document executed out of India is brought to the Collector after expiry of 3
months with the plea that it was omitted to be stamped early by mistake, the Collector may if he is
satisfied that the omission to stamp it has been occasionéd by accident, mistake or urgent necessity,
proceed under Sections 41 and 42 if it is within the period of one year from the date of execution
(Note 2 under Section 18). Otherwise it should be impoundéd under Section 33 and dealtwith under
Sections 40 and 41 " : :

CLARIFICATION: The Commissioner and Inspector General, in his Circular Memo No.S5/
5565/97 Dt.24-9-1997 clarified that the stamps purchased in Andhra Pradesh relating to property
situated in Andhra Pradesh though executed outside India are still valid irrespective of its place of
its execution

Section.19: The section applies to Bills and Promissory Notes drawn out of India
PRONOTE MADE OUT OF INDIA

The. first holder in India shall, before he presents for payment or endorses, transfers or
otherwise negotiate the Pronote, affix the proper duty to it and cancel the same

Section.19A: The section applies to instruments executed out of Andhra Pradesh in India and has
become chargeable with a higher rate of duty in A-P. under clause (b) of Sec.3.

Instruments executed in other States in India and thereafter received in the State of A.P.
shall be chargeable with the difference if any between the duty chargeable in this State and the
duty already paid on the instrument in the State in which it was executed.
238

Section.20: The Section provides’ the procedure and ‘manner to be adopted for conversion of
amounts expressed in Foreign Currencies.

__ Where the value in an instrument is expressed in Dollars, the, duty shall be calculated.on
the value of the Dollars in Indian Currency according to the current rate of Exchange as.on the
date of the instrument.

Section.21: The section enjoins how the stock of Marketable Sectrrities are to be valued,

Where an instrument relates to marketable securities, the duty shall be calculated on the
value of the ‘said securities according to their average price on the date of the instrument.

Section.22: The section explains the effect of the statement of rate of Exchange or Average
price.

Where an instrument contains a Statement of current rate of Exchange or “Average price
as the case may require and is stamped in accordance with such statement., it shall}‘so far as
regards the subject matter of such statement be presumed, until the contrary is Proved, to be duly
stamped. > : y

Section.23: The section explains the duty payable on instruments reserving initeresi.

Section.23A: ‘The section provides for payment of stamp duty on instruments of Mortgage of
Marketable securities and Release of mortgage of marketable sécurities.

It applies to certain instruments ‘connected with Mortgage of marketable securities to be
chargeable as’ Agreements, ~

(a) The section requires that the instttinent should not be a Promissory note or a Bill of
Exchange or

«-(b) That it:should be given’ on the occasion of a‘deposit of Marketable Security by way of
security or

(c) That it may make redeemable or qualify a duly stamped transfer intended as security of
any marketable security. |

A Releaseor discharge of any such instrument is chargeable with the duty as an Agreement.

Section.24: The Section provides for deduction of stamp duty on an instrument of sale of property
subject to mortgage debt, is sold to’ the mortgagee under certain circumstances (Sale i in favour of
mortgagee).

The duty is payable on the consideration including the amount of debt to be discharged or
amount agreed to be paid or the market value of the property whichever is higher.

The second proviso to Sec.24 applies to documents by which:
‘(1) « The Mortgage is extinguished

(2) The Sale must be by the mortgagor or his heirs or legal representatives ora person
who acquited the mottgaged property from the mortgager

Be The Sale must be in favour of the mortgagee or toa person to whom the mortgage is
transferred
239
(4) Whole or part of the property subject to mortgage should be transferred ,
(5) If only part of the mortgaged property is sold, other property should not be included.

Section.25; The section provides the manner as to how valuation, in case of annuity, has to be
determined.

Where in an instrument the consideration is “Annuity” payable for a period of 50 years, the
duty shall be calculated on the total amount to be paid for 50 years (Sec.25 (a)).

When the stamp duty has to be paid on the basis of the annuity payable, the value has to be,
calculated as follows.

a) When the annuity is payable for a On such total amount
definite period

b) Where the sum is payable in On total amount which may be payable
perpetuity or for an indefinite term during the period of twenty years
not terminable with any life in being calculated from the date on which the
on the date of the instrument first payment becomes due

c) Where the sum is payable for On the maximum which will or may be
indefinite term terminable with any payable as aforesaid during the period of
life in being on the date of instrument twelve years calculated from the date on

which the first payment becomes due.

Section.26: The section governs fevy of stamp duty on Mining leases and applies as to the duty
required on instruments where value of the subject matter is indeterminate.

The Section lays down procedure for stamping in respect of mining lease granted by
government or when the lease is granted by any other person. This section applies only when the
instrument is liable to advalorem duty. ,

This section should be read subject to section 35, whenever, the claim exceeds the amount
proportionate to the stamp, the document is not duly stamped for the purpose for which it was
executed within the meaning of section 35 and the provision of this section would apply thereto it.

Section.27; The section requires that the circumstances and all other facts affecting chargeability
under Stamp Act of an instrument with duty should be fully and truly setforth in the instrument.

The Market value of the property dealtwith in the instrument should.be fully and truly set
forth (A.P. Act 22 of 1971). .

The person (Donor) against whom prosecution was sanctioned for the offence of
underyaluation was dead. No prosecution lies against the claimant unless the case falls under
Section 64(b). (Note u/s 27).

If in a Gift deed the value of the property is not mentioned aud the donor died, the value
should the ascertained from the donee and the deed stamped accordingly. If the donee refuses, to
stamp the deed, action should be taken under section 64 to prosecute him (Note u/s 27 A.P.S.M)
240

A more common method of evading stamp duty is by undervaluation. Such an act
contravenes section 27 punishable under section 64.But it is:very.difficult'to prove intention to
defraud Government which is essential to secure Prosecution. The introduction of Section 47-A
curbed this tendency to some extent. But even under this scheme. correct particulars of ‘Buildings
are being suppressed to evade payment of proper duty,

Section.28: The Section provides for splitting the transaction in to two or more. instruments to
have the benefit of payment of less Stamp duty. '

When property agreed to be sold is conveyed in parts through Separate instruments, market
value of each part should be stated arid the duty is payable on the Consideration’or Market Value
whichever is higher (A.P. Act 22 ‘of 1971). ° e

Section.30: The Section makes it obligatory on a person to give a duty stamped Receipt provided
there’is a demand for the same by the person paying or delivering money exceeding Rs. 500/-
relating to any Bill, Cheque, Promissory Note or Property. If he refuses to grant such a Receipt, he
will be liable to prosecution under Section 65 of the Act.

Section.31: for adjiidi sc stamp duty payablé/on' documents
‘ pinion of the Collector.

     
  

 

There is no time limit for seeking the opinion of the Collector under section 3 £. The time limit
is only for adding certificate under Section 32.

After adjudication of.the document, and giving opinion under section 31, the Collector
(Registrar) becomes functus officio. He should not therefore impound the document as the
adjudication was made under section 31.

There is no objection to the presentation and adjudication of documents under Section 31 in
a district different from that in-which they were executed or the property concerned was situated
(Note 2 under Section 31), Any person can apply for adjudication and it is not necessary that party
to the document alone should apply. But by an administrative order the District Registrars were
instructed to entrtain such applications in respect of instruments relating to properties situated in
their respective districts.

Such document cannot be impounded. .

or any other officer appointed :as. Collector: under section '31 of the Actialongwith'the original
document and a copy of it, for adjudication. The Collector shall collect adjudication, fe
under the section and may call for any additional information | or.an affidavit to prove
facts and circumstances, affecting the chargeability of the instrument have been ‘truly and fully
setfotth in'the document. The Collector will then. determine-the duty. If the.document'is already

document. If the proper duty determined or deficit portion thercof is paid, he shall certify that the
full duty with which it was char} geable was paid, If however, the party does not pay the
the case falls under the proviso (a) to (c) of section 32, the Collector should hot add thé Cerlificate.
He shall return the document with his opinion alone through a letter. (Section 31.& 32) He cannot

it was’executed out of India . .

Section.33: The section deals with the examination and impounding of instruments ‘by Officers
incharge of public offices. Revenue Inspector and Head-Accountants are-not persons in charge of
public office. So they cannot impound documents (Note 3 and 7. under section 33):/..3 2

Section.34: The section provides for substitution of unstamped receipts with duly stamped receipts
when such stamps are produced in audit,

_ Unstamped receipts produced in evidence may be admitted in evidence on payment of fine
of Rs.3/- (Vide section 35(b)).

Section.35: The section provides as to how instruments not duly stamped produced in evidence
are to be dealtwith for realization of the Stamp Revenue.

There is no provision to levy deficit duty and penalty on the basis of the Certified copy
(Note 4 u/s 35).

Stamp duty should be determined with reference to the recitals in the document, If the
delegation as to the presentation of P-T.A. is silent in a Power of Attorney, the Sub Register should
not accept P.T.A from the agent and should not impound the Power of Attorney (Note'13 under
Sec.35).

Deficit stamp duty and penalty cannot.be collected when the party contends that the
document was a forgery (Note 6 u/s 35).

Documents insufficiently stamped and produced in the enquiries under Sections 71,72 and
73 of the Registration Act before the Registrar, have to be dealtwith u/s35 only.

In the case of unstamped Receipts produced in evidence in Courts or Public offices, it is
enough if the penalty alone is levied. Deficit duty need not be collected (Note 12 u/s 35).

Section.36: The Section refers to all instruments which have not been duly stamped but admitted
in evidence erroneously not to be questioned.

The Section refers to all Instruments that have been admitted in evidence, whether on
payment of the deficit duty and penaity or on the ground that they have been duly stamped or not
chargeable with stamp duty. Such admission cannot be called in question at any stage of the suit or
proceedings on ground of insufficiency of stamp..[AIR 1962 Ker 265 (266)].

Section.37: The section refers to admission of improperly stamped instruments. This is a remedial
section. |

The section read with Rule 18 relates to validation of documents bearing stamps of proper
value but of improper description.

Section.40: The Section applies to instruments impounded u/s33 or serit'to the Collector u/s38(2).
This section governs levy of deficit stamp duty and penalty on insufficiently stamped documents.

The law does not contemplate the imposition of maximum penalty as‘ matter of course.
The Collector should give reasons for levying maximum penalty (Note 13 u/s. 40 A.P.S.M)

Pronotes, Bill of Exchange and Receipts, if not duly stamped, cannot be validated under
sections 40 & 42. They should be permanently impounded and destroyéd after 12 years (Note It
Under Sec. 40) from the date of their execution ( Page 343 of A.P.S.M)

Section:41: The section applies to the “not duly stamped” documents due to accident, ‘mistake or
urgent necessity. It governs levy of deficit duty on documents voluntarily produced within one
year from the date of execution.

The adjudication of documents under section 41 will not-apply to:documents presented.
after one year from the date of their execution

‘No Penalty is leviable for adjudication of documents under Section41

Section.41A: The section was newly incorporated which came into force on 16- 8- 1986. (Act No
17/1986). The section is reproduced hereunder.

“ 41-A Recavery of Stamp duty not levied or short levied.:- Where after the
commencement of the Indian'Stamp Act. (Andhra Pradesh Amendment) Act, 1986, any instrument
chargeable with duty has not been duly stamped, and registered by any Registering Officer by
mistake and remarked as such by the Collector or any Audit party, the Collector may, within five
245

years from, the date of registration serve a notice,on the person by. whom the duty was payable
requiring him to show cause why the proper duty or the amount required to make up the same
should not be collected form him:

Provided that where the non-payment was, by reason of fraud, collusion or any. willful mis-
statement or suppression of facts or contravention of any Of the provisions of this Act or the tules
“Tiade thereunder with intent to evade payment of duty, the Collector may within ten years from
the date of registration serve'a notice on such oersib to cause why the amount required to ‘mak up
the deficit stamp duty should not be collected from him along. with a penalty of three times of the
aefict stamp: duty, (Act No 16/2002)__ oO i

 

@) The Collector or any officer specially authorized by him in this behalf shall, after considering

the representation if any, made by the person on whom notice is served under sub-section(1),
determine by an Order, the amount of duty and penalty due from such person (not being in excess
of the amount specified in.the notice) and thereupon, such person shall pay the amount as determined.
On payment.of the amount, the Collector shall add.a certificate under Section 42:

(3) Any person aggrieved by an order under sub-section(2) may prefer an appeal before the
Chief Controlling Revenue Authority, Andhra Pradesh, Hyderabad, within three months from the
date of such orders.

(4).. Any amount payable under this section shall be recovered as an arrear of land Revenue.

Deficit-stamp duty and penalty can be collected by coercive process only from the: person
liable to pay the. duty as provided for in section 29.

In respect of document insufficiently stamped and presented to a Sub Registrar for
registration, the presentant shall be given an opportunity to get it validated under sections 41 at the
time of presentation of the.document as the Sub Registrar himself may collect the deficit stamp
duty and certify it under section 42 as he is appointed as “Collector” under section 41.”

This section provides for collection of the Revenue lost on account..of registration of
insufficiently stamped documents remarked by the Collector or any Audit party by serving notice
within five years from the date of registration and where non payment of duty on an instrument
was by reason of fraud. collusion or any willful misstatement or suppression of facts or contravention
of any of the provisions of the Act and the rules made’ thereunder with intent to evade payment
of duty, by issue of notice by the Collector within ten years from the date of registration, on the
person liable to pay the duty under section 29. After collection, a Certificate under Section 42 shal]
be added on the document concerned if available.

Sub section 4 provides for an appeal by the person aggrieved by the orders of the Collector
demanding payment.

Seetion.44: The ‘séction’ provides for recovery of the’ duty dha! penalty. pai
bound to pay in respect of an instrument under section’ 35,;37,40'or 41 If some’ other person’ was
251

bound to bear the expense of providing the proper stamp for such instrument was paid by the first
mentioned person, he is entitled to recover from such other person , the duty and penalty so paid.

The section is intended to give relief to an innocent party, who was'made liable for the
payment of the deficiency in duty and penalty, to recover from the real defaulter. The:idea underlying
the section is that the party producing an instrument in evidence may not be the person responsible
in law for properly stamping it and that he might have paid the duty and penalty under section 35
and as such the séction enables him to recover it form the person responsible to duly stamp the
instrument. The recovery of such amount should be effected by.a separate suit.

Section.45: The section enables for refund of the stamp duty and penalty collected in excess of
legal requirements.

(1) The powers of the C.CR.A under this section have been delegated to District Collectors
(G.0.2911 S.R.6-10-1914 BP 238/1299, R.M is 26-10-1914)

(2) The powers of refund given to the C.C.R.A is discretionary and no further proceedings
are maintainable if the application for refund is refused. The High Court has no jurisdiction
to entertain the question of stamp duty and penalty illegally realized by the Collector
from the party under the Stamp Act. -

(3) Application for refund of the stamp duty charged in excess and paid shall be made within
three months from the-date of the order charging the same. Application. for refund of
penalty is to be made within one year from the date of payment.

(4) Acase of voluntary or mistaken payment of stamp duty does not fall under. sub section(2).

Section.46: This section protects the person who has impounded an instrument from any liability
arising from the loss or destruction of or damage to the instrument while being sent to the Collector
(in transit). Sub section 2 authorises, the Impounding Officer to demand a copy of the document
before impounding to serve as a safe guard against the risk of loss for the benefit of such person.
The copy so made should be-retained in the custody of the Impounding Officer.

Section.47: The:section relates to stamping of Bills of Exchange and Promissory notes not duly
stamped. :

The section lays down that a person to whom a Bill of Exchange or Promissory Note not
duly stamped is presented for payment, the payee, without refusing the same, should affix stamp of
the necessary value to such instruments arid then make payment. The stamp so affixed should be
cancelled before making payment.

Section.47-A:. The section is newly introduced effective from 16-8-1986 (Act.No 17/1986). The
stamp duty in respect of sales is payable on the consideration or the market value of the property
conveyed whichever is higher. In respect of Gift, Exchange, Partition, settlement and Release, it is
payable on the Market value of the property. If it is not so paid, the Registering Officer shall make
a reference to the Collector before registering the document. The Collector determines the market
value after following the procedure prescribed and collect the difference in duty if any .If the party
is aggrieved he can appeal to the civil court.

business was done on slip or unstamped Policics.

Failure to issue duly stamped Insurance Policy is an offence.

general provisions of section 62.

Failure to execute full number of duly stamped Bills of Exchange and Maririe Insurance
Policies is an offence.

Sections 66 and 67 create special offences in respect of Insurance Policies.

Promissory Notes.

Post dating bill with a view to deprive Government of the revenue is an offence.

Section.69: Disobedience of rules by licensed stamp vendors and sale of stamps by unlicensed
vendors is an offence.

When a licensed vendor purchases stamps from a private person he is liable for punish-
ment for abatement of an offence under section 69(b)(Note 3 under section 69).

on the record.

CHAPTER -2
DEFINITIONS
Adhesive Stamp:- There shall be two kinds of stamps for indicating the payment of duty on

instruments namely :- (a) impressed stamps and (b) adhesive stamps (Rule 3). The adhesive
stamps may be used for the documents mentioned in Sec.11 and Rules 13 to 17.

Adjudication:- Adjudication is a legal act of determination by “Collector” of stamp duty payable
on an instrument under Sec. 31 and 40 of the Act.

Adoption Deed:- The ceremony of giving the child in adoption and taking the child in adoption
is called adoption. A deed recording this ceremony is called a deed of adoption. For the purpose
of payment of stamp duty, an authority to adopt also was included in the definition of adoption

-deed, Art 3. of Sch.1A. Vide Serial No.5 under chapter 3.

Affidavit:- Affidavit is a written statement on oath. It includes an affirmation or declaration in
the case of persons allowed by law to affirm or declare instead of swearing. Art. 4 of Sch. LA.

Agreement:- An agreement is an instrument whereby the parties willfully agree to perform
certain acts or refrain from doing something unilaterally or bilaterally.

Annuity:- Annuity is the amount payable every year. When it is payable for a definite period
the duty should be paid on the total amount. When it is not terminable with any life in being on
the date of document it is payable on the amounts payable during 20 years. When it is terminable
with any life, it is payable on the amounts payable during 12 years. Sec. 25,

Additional Security:- Where additional property is offered as security for the debt already
received through a duly stamped deed of mortgage by way of further assurance, it is called
‘Additional Security’ falling under Art. 35(c) of Sch. IA. The duty is subject to a maximum of
Rs. 30 vide item 51, Notification No 13, dated 17-12-1938.

Award:- It is a decision in writing by an arbitrator or umpire. For the purpose of Stamp Act,
award directing partition should be stamped as a deed of partition. Further, awards made on a
reference by an order of court are not chargeable with stamp duty. Article 12. of Sch. 1A.

Bottomry Bond:- It is an instrument where the Master of a sea-going ship borrows money on
the security of the ship to enable him to preserve the ship or prosecute her voyage. (Art. 14).
This Article does not apply to bottomry bonds given in respect of ships referred to in proviso
(2); Under Sec. (3).

10. Bond:- Bond includes

a) any instrument whereby a person obliges himseif to pay money to another, on
condition that the obligation shall be void if a specified act is performed, or is not
performed, as the case may be
258

b) any instrument attested by.a witness and not payable to, order or-bearer. whereby
a person obliges himself to pay money to another and...

: c) any instrument so attested, whereby a person obliges himself to deliver grain or
other ‘agricultufal ptoduce to another. See 2(5). : :

First is called conditional bond. The sécond and third are called simple bond. The
important word in the definition-is “obliges”. Therefore no documerit can:bé:a bond.unless it
‘creates an express-obligation to pay. “

1. Chargeable:- The word “chargeable” means chargeable under the Stamp Act in force « on the
“date of the execution of the i instrument, or the first execution as the case ‘may be. Sec. 2(6)

12. Chief Controlling Revenue Authority: (CG R.A):+ The definition of. this expression was
omitted from the Act i in 1937. According to General. Clauses Act, it means:-

(a): } sin a State where there is Board. of Revenue = that Board
) in a State where there is Revenue Commissioner - that
Commissioner.
” Note: In the A: P. the Commissioner and Inspector General ‘of Registration and Stamps j is

designated as C.C.R.A.

13. Chit Agreement:- It is an instrument by which a foreman e enters into an agreement with one

~“ of more humber of subscribers that every one of them shall subscribe 'a cértain sunv‘of money

etc;, by instalments and that ‘each of them shall be entitled to a prize amount by participating i in
“auction ‘Art. 18(2) tead with Sec. 20): of A.P. Chit Funds Act 197.

4. Codicil:- Codicil, isa supplement toa will, When a testator wishes to make some alteration i in
his will, he may do so by executing a Codicil to his will. All the provisions. of law which apply
“toa will equally apply to the Codicil. .

15. Co-Owner:- Where a property is held in undivided shares by joint tenants, tenants i in common
“or Co- Pareeners, they are’ called co-owners.” *

16. Counter Part:- Where several parts of an insteument are re interchangeably executed by several
parties, that part of copy which is executed by the grantor is usually called the original and the
rest are counterparts.

17. Collector:- “Collector” means the District Collector and includes any. officer ‘whom the State
Government may appoint bya notification i in the e Official Garette Vide Sec. 200).

The; Power to appoint.Collectors was delegated to the C.C.R.A

Vide Chapter 2 for list of R&S dept. officers appointed as “Collectors”.
18.

20.

21.

22.

25.

259

Composition Deed:- It is an instrument executed by a debtor whereby he conveys the property
for the benefit of his creditors or whereby payment of composition or dividend is secured to the
creditors or whereby provision is made fur the continuance of the debtor's business under the
supervision of Inspectors for the benetit of the creditors. Art. 19. of Sch 1A.

There should not be a direct transfer of the property to the creditors.

Compounding:- Chapter VII of the Stamp Act prescribes penalties for the various offences
under the Stamp Act. Sec. 70(1) lays down that no prosecution for such offence shail be
instituted without the sanction of the Collector or the authorised officer. Sec. 70(2) empowers
the C.C.R.A. or any authorised officer to stay such prosecution or compound any such offence.
Compounding of offence means condoning of the offence on payment of composition fee
mutually agreed by the party and the compounding officer.

Conveyance:- Conveyance includes a conveyance on sale and every instrument by which
property, movable or immovable is transferred inter vivos and which is not other wise specifically
provided for in Sch. I or Sch I-A as the case may be. { Sec.2(10) )

Debenture:- Debenture is a document or a certificate signed by the officer of a Corporation
or Company acknowledging indebtedness for money lent and guaranteeing repayment with
interest usually creating a charge on the whole or part of the property of the company.

It is chargeable under Art. 27 of Schedule-I.

Denoting Duty:- Where the duty with which an instrument is chargable or its exemption from.
duty depends upon the duty paid in respect of another instrument, the payment of such last
mentioned duty shall, on application being made to the Collector and on production of both the
instruments be denoted on the first mentioned instrument by endorsement by the Collector.
This is called “Denoting Duty “. (Sec. 16).

Divorce:- It is an instrument by which any person effects the dissolution of his/her marriage.
(Art. 25. of Sch 14.)

Dower:- A deed of Dower is a settlement of property in consideration of, or on the occasion
of marriage between muslims. The former is chargeable as a settlement while the latter is
exempt from duty. (Art. 49. of Sch 1A.)

Duly Stamped:- Duly stamped means that the instrument bears an adhesive or impressed
stamp of not less than the proper amount and that such stamp has been affixed or used in
accordance with the law for the time being in force in India. Sec. 211).

English Mortgage:- Where the mortgagor binds himself to pay the mortgage money ona
certain date and transfers the mortgaged property absolutely to the mortgagee, but subject to
a proviso that he will retransfer it to the mortgagor upon payment of the mortgage money as
agreed, the transaction is called an English Mortgage, Vide also S.No.43 (Sec. 58(e) T-P. Act.)
260:

27.. Exchange:- When two.persons mutually transfer the ownership of one thing for.the.owner
stip of another, neither thing nor both things being money, the:transaction is called.an Exchange.
(See. 118 of TP.Act.):: 7 Me : '

   

28. Executed And Execution :- ‘Executed’ and ‘Execution’ used with reference to instruments
: mean ‘signed and signature’ (Sec. '2(12).) =

 

‘Execution’ means ‘completed’ and the term ‘Execution’ when aj toa document is
the last act which completes it. It includes attestation Wherever it is necessary. Signature
includes mark by an illiterate person. Pals

29. Ex-Officio Stamp Vendor:- The Treasury Officer.or such other officers of Government as
the Board of Revenue or, Government may appoint to sell stamps in local, branch or sub depot,
are called Ex-officio Stamp ‘Vendors. :

Rule.27 of Rules forSupply.and Distribution of Stamps and BSOs 604. and OOB...

30. Further Charge:- It is an instriiment imposing a further charge on property already. risrtgaged
in favour of the sartie person. A second mortgage iti favour of another person is not a a further
charge (Article 28, of Sch 1A.) S Bee

31; Functus. Officio:- When the court or officer complétes the discharge of the function ‘with
“regard to any matter’ pending ‘before it/him it is called ‘“Functtis officio” ‘

 

oy». When a deficitly stamped document i is presented for adjudication to the Collector under
Sec, 31, the. Collector becomes: functus, officio after he determines .,the duty payable. ‘He cannot
therefore impound the said document even if the deficit duty.i is not paid... 5 clowns

When a Registering officer registers.a,document he becomes functus officio. He cannot
thereafter impound the document even if he discovers later that was not duly stamped.

32. Gift:- Gift is the transfer of certain existing movable or immovable property made voluntarily
and without consideration by the dénior to the donee and accepted by or on behalf of the donee
during the life time of the donor. Sec. 204) of the Stamp Act falls under thé definition of Gift
under T.P. Act.

» So: gifts: falling under Sec: 2(24) of the: Stamp Act: should .be-chargéd under Art. 49
(Settlement ) and others alone under Article.29(Gift).

33. Impounding:- The word “impound” means to confine, not-to return. Every. person having
authority to receive evidence and-every pes son‘in'charge of. public office except-an officer of
police, before whom any instrument chargeable with duty is produced in the performance of
his functions shall impound it if it appears to him that it is not duly stamped. Sec: 33.

The court/officer cannot impound the document after it/ he becomes functous officio nor
can the document be impounded unless it was-produced in evidence or in‘ perforinancé of the
duties: of the officer.; -.;.

Impressed Labels:-'The instruments mentioned in Sec. 1i and Rule 13 may-be stamped
with adhesive stamps by the parties themselves eg: Pronotes. The instruments mentioned in
Rule 17 may be stamped by the parties themselves with special adhesive stamps. e.g Foreign
Bill, Share Transfer.

The documents mentioned in Annexure- II and HI to Indian Stamp Rules 1925 may be
affixed with labels and impressed by the Proper Officers mentioned in Annexure ~ I,in the
manner prescribed in Rules 10 and 11.

Impressed Stamps:- “Impressed Stamp” includes -
(a) Labels affixed and impressed by the Proper Officer and

(b) | Stamps embossed or engraved on stamped paper. Sec.2(13) Appendix — I to Indian
Stamp Rules contains the list of “Proper Officers “ referred to above.

Indemnity Bond:- A contract of indemnity is a contract by which one party promises to save
the other from loss caused to him by the conduct of the promisor himself or. the conduct of any
other person. (Sec, 124 of Indian Contracts.Act.) When the contract is. in the form of bond it
is chargeable under Art. 30. of Sch LA.

Instrument:- ‘Instrument’ includes every document by which any right, or liabilityis, or purports
to be, created, transferred, limited, extended, extinguished or recorded (Sec. 2(14).) The definition
is not exhaustive. Affidavits and copies etc., which do not create any right are also included in
Schedules as instruments. Unsigned draft document is not an instrument. A rectification deed
which does not create right or liability or does not purports to be created, transferred, limited,
extended, extinguished or recorded etc., any right is not instrument.

Lease:- Lease means lease of immovable property, It includes —
a) A patta.

b) A kabuliyat or undertaking in writing, not being a counterpart of a lease, to cultivate,
occupy or pay or deliver for immovable property.

c) Any instrument by which tolls are let,

d) Any writing on an application for a lease intended to signify that the application is
granted. Sec. 2(16).

A lease of immovable property is a transfer of a right to enjoy such property in
consideration of a price paid or promised or money, a share of crops, service or,any other thing
of value to be rendered periodically or on specified occasions to the transferor by the transferee
(vide Sec. 105 of T.P. Act.)

Instruments which may not come under definition of lease under T.P. Act may amount
to lease under the Stamp Act.

Immovable property is not defined in Stamp Act. According to General Clauses Act it
includes growing crops and grass though under TP. Act , it dose not.

Mortgage deed as defined in Sec.58 of T.P. Act applies to immovable property alone while
the definition in Stamp Act applies to movable and immovable properties. T.P. Act makes a
separate provision in Sec. 100 for charges over immovable property. Such.charges are included
in the definition of mortgage in Stamp Act. (A.P High Court judgement dt. 23-7-1985 in referred
case No. 112/1979. I.G’s file SA1/32267/87 dt. 1-1-1988).

Marketable Security:- Marketable Security means a security of such a description as to be
capable of being sold in any stock market in India or in the United Kingdom (Sec. 2(16A).)

Mortgage by Deposit of Title Deeds:- Where a person, in any of the Notified towns
delivers to the creditor or his agent documents of title to immovable property with intent to
create a security thereon. The transaction is called a mortgage by deposit of title deeds. (Section
§8(f) T.P. Act.) It is also called an equitable mortgage. A deed by which such deposit is made
is chargeable under Article 7. If the deed creates an interest not only in the title deeds but also
in the properties themselves it is chargeable as a deed of mortgage under Article 35(b). of Sch.
1A.

Munigutta Cowl:- Munigutta Cow! is a form of usufructuary mortgage whereby the owner
of the property transfers it to the mortgageee (Lessee) as security for the money advanced
and to be enjoyed for a certain period towards the principal and interest.

Notarial Act:- Any instrument , endorsement, note, attestation, certificate or entry not being a
protest (Art43), made or signed by a notary in execution of his duties or by any other person
lawfully acting as a Notary (Art 37 of Sch 1A).

Notary:- A Notary is a public duly appointed officer who is under the Notaries Act whose
function is to draw, attest and certify deeds under his office seal to note or certify transaction
relating to Negotiable Instruments and to draw up protests by Master of Ship.

Partnership:- Partnership is the relationship between persons who have agreed to share the
business carried on by all or any of them acting for all (Sec. 4 of Indian Partnership Act). The
stamp duty is payable under Art. 41-A of Sch. 1A. :

Partition:- Instrument of partition means any instrument whereby co-owners of any property
divide or agree to divide such property in severalty. It includes a final order for effecting a
partition passed by any Revenue authority or any Civil Court. Jt also includes an award by an
arbitrator directing a partition. Sec.2(15).

Pawn or Pledge:- It is an instrument by which there is actual or constructive delivery of
movable property as security for the repayment of money advanced or to be advanced by way
of loan or an existing or future debt. (Art 7(2)) If possession is not given, it is hypothecation of
movable property.

Premium:- Premium is a price paid or promised by the lessee to the lessor in consideration of
the lease (Sec. 105 of T-P. Act.) Value of improvements cannot be Tegarded as premium.
(AIR 1967 AP90(FB).

64, Security Bond:- The following 3 classes of Security bonds alone fall under Art. 48. Security

There can be no contract between the court and parties to the suit. Hence security

65. Separated Share:- The largest share remaining after the property is partitioned or if there

66. Settlement:- settlement means a non testamentary disposition in writing of movable or

(a) Inconsideration of marriage.

(b) For the purpose of distributing the property of the settlor among his family or
those for whom he desires to provide or for providing for some person dependent
on him or

(c) For any religious or charitable purpose.

It includes an agreement in writing to make such a disposition. A deed of trust falls
under the definition of Settlement unless the property was already vested in the
beneficiary. (Sec. 2 (24).) If not so a vested, the document falls under the detinition.
of Gift under Sec. 122 of T.P. Act. Hence such documents are classified in the

departmental accounts as Gift (Settlement) and chargeable to Stamp Duty as
“Settlements” only.

267
CHAPTER -3
DISTINCTIONS BETWEEN TERMS

Assignment of Lease and Transfer of Lease:- Both the terms convey the same meaning.
Transfer of lease can be two ways, assignment and under lease or sub lease. In the case of
assignment of lease, the lessee transfers all his rights under the original lease to the transferee
who will be answerable to the original lessor. It is chargeable under Art.54 of Sch LA. Under-
lease or sublease falls under Art. 31 of Sch LA. In this, the sub lessee is to pay the rent to the
original lessee.

Agreement relating to deposit of title deeds and mortgage by deposit of title deeds:-
Both the classes of documents are chargeable under Art. 7 of Sch. I-A. They are also called
equitable mortgages. They are executed to secure repayment of money by the deposit of title
déeds evidencing the title to any property other than marketable security. Vide also S.No.46
under definitions - Chapter 2 for more elcidation. :

Adhesive Stamp and Revenue Stamp:- Revenue Stamps are adhesive stamps bearing the
inscription “India Revenue” of the denominations of Rs.1/-, 25 Ps.. 15.Ps., LOPs., and 5 Paise.
They are used for instruments on which stamp duty cannot be paid for the reason that the
necessary stamps are not in circulation. (Rule 13-A and Rule 16) They are sold through post
offices but the Revenue goes to the State Government. Unlicensed vendors can sell these
stamps (Section 69(b).)

Annuity Bond and Annuity Mortgage:- An instrument by which a person obliges to pay a
certain amount periodically to another person or persons is an annuity bond chargeable under
Art. 13 read with Sec. 25 of Sch LA. Whereas in such a document, if property is offered as
security for such payment, it is chargeable as Annuity Mortgage under Art. 35 read with
Section 25 of Sch. LA. ,

Adoption deed and Authority to Adopt:- Adoption deed is an instrament recording the act
of taking a child in adoption. It is a non-testamentary document registerable in Book 4. An
authority to adopt is a deed by which the husband authorises his wife to. adopt a child. It is a
testamentary document registerable in Book 3. An adoption deed does not require registration
unless it creates a right in immovable property. An authority to adopt is compulsorily register-
able. (Section 17(3) Registration Act).

Articles of Association and Memorandum of Association:- A Memorandum of Associa-
tion contains. the objects for which the company is being incorporated, while the Articles of
Association contain the detailed provisions for the working of the Company. The former is
chargeable under Article 34 while the later under Article 11 of Sch 1A.

Adjudication and Impounding:- Adjudication is the act of determination by the ‘Collector’
of the stamp duty payable on an instrument (Sec. 31 and 40) while impounding is detaining a
deficitly stamped document by the court or officer before whom it is produced in performance
of its or his duties.
268

Acknowledgement and Bond:- An acknowledgement (Art. Di is executed merely to supply

“ evidence of debt. It should not contain any promise to repay it or'to pay interest, whereas a
“bond contains ari express obligation to pay inoney, grain or other agricultural produce to an-

other (Sec. 2(5) and Art. 13. of Sch 1A.)

Bond and agreement:- Bond contains an express obligation to pay money or deliver grain
etc. The obligation to pay is chief covenant in Bond whereas in'an Agreement bréach of the
Contract is compensated i in damages. However an unattested bond Should be treated as an
agreement. (Sec. 2(5)).

Bond and Mortgage:- Bond is a personal obligation to repay a debt while mortgage is’ a
transfer of interest in specified property as security for repayment of debt, or for Performance
of an engagement. (Section 2(5) and Sec..2(17)).:

Bond falls under Article 13 and Mortgage under Article 35 of Sch. IA.

Bond and Pronote:- . Pronote i isa negotiable i instrument while Bond is not. Pronote j is payable
to order or bearer but bond is not so payable. Bond requires attestation while pronote need not
be attested. Pronote contains an undertaking to pay money only. Bond may contain an under-
taking to deliver grain or other agricultural produce also. (See. 265); Sec. 2(22)). Duty in
respect of Bond is payable under Art: 13 of Sch. I- A. while duty on’ Pronote i is 8 payable under

“Art. 49 of Sch. I(Central Liat).

Cancellation and Revocation:~ Cancellation (Art. 15) is a residuary article. Cancellation not
otherwise’ provided for falls under this Article. Cancellations specifical ly. provided for are
Releasés, Surrendar of leasé, Revocation of Settlement and Revocation of Trust. Cancellation
of Settlements and Trusts are called Revocations.

Conveyance and Composition Deed:- In a conveyance there is a diréct transfer of prop-
erty by the debtor to the creditor whereas in a composition deed, the property of the debtor is

- conveyed to the trustee for the benefit of the creditors, Duty on conveyance is payable under
