Co-owners (Joint Owners ).& Co-Parceners:- Persons jointly owning property or tenants
in common are called co-owners or joint owners. e.g, partners in a firm:or persons who pur-
chased or acquired property jointly. Members of joint family owning properties are,called co-
parceners.

Copy and Duplicate:- A duplicate is an:exact reproduction of the original containing the
original signature of parties-and witnesses. While a copy also in-anexact reproduction of the
original, it does not contain the original signatures of parties and witnesses. It'is certified as a
copy by the authorised officer.

Counterpart and Duplicate:- Counterpart = See Chapter 2 S. No. 16: ‘Duplicate is is‘an exact
reproduction of the original, except with regard to stamp duty. |

Debenture and Share certificate:- Debenture is an instrument evidencing the debt due to
the debenture holder by the company while a share certificate evidences the title of the holder
to a share in the company. A debenture holder is entitled to fixed rate of interest while a share
holder is entitled to dividend which is fluctuating.

Declaration of Trust and Transfer of Trust:- See S.No. 78 of Chapter 2. Transfer of trust
is an instrument whereby trust property is transferred from one trustee or Board of trustees to
another trustee or Board or to the beneficiary

Declaration of Trust is chargeable under Article 55 A and Transfer of trust under
Article 53(b). of Sch. 1A.

Duly Stamped and properly Stamped:- Duly stamped means that an instrument bears an
adhesive or impressed stamp of not less than the proper amount and that such stamp has been
affixed in accordance with the law for the time being in force (Sec.2(11)). When an instrument
bears a’ stamp of proper value but of improper description it is said to be improperly stamped.
(Sec: 37).

Earnest Money and Premium:- Earnest money is an amount advanced in consideration of a
contract which is adjustable in the final consideration. Premium is a price paid or payable by
the lessee to the lessor in consideration of the lease (Sec. 105, T.P. Act) It is neither adjustable
in rent, nor repayable.

English Mortgage and Mortgage by conditional Sale:- English Mortgage is an ostensible
sale. In an English Mortgage the mortgagor transfers the property absolutely to the Mortgagee
subject to retransfer on repayment of the mortgage money.

In amortgage by conditional sale the mortgagor ostensibly sells the property to the
mortgagee subject to the following conditions.

(a) On default of payment of the mortgage money by a certain date. the sale shall
become absolute.

(b) On such repayment being made the sale shall become void.

(c) On such repayment being made the buyer shall retransfer the property to the
‘seller, (Sec. S8(C) and (e) T.P. Act).

Ex-Officio Vendor and Licensed Stamp Vendor:- The Treasury Officer or such other
officer of the Government as the Board of Revenue or Government may appoint shall be ex-
officio Vendor of all descriptions of stamps in local depot. (Rule 27 of Rules for Supply and
Distribution of Stamps). All Sub-Treasury officers are. also-Ex-officio Vendors (BSO. 59).
Some of the Sub Registrars are appointed as Ex-officio Vendors (BSO 65). Private parties are
licensed by District Registrars to sell stamps (BSO 77(1)(iii)). In the twin cities of Hyderabad
and Secunderabad the Assistant Inspector General of Stamps is the licensing authority. They
are called licensed Stamp Vendors.

Further . charge and fresh Mortgage:- Some times the same mortgagee advances further
sums of money. to the mortgagor on the same. security and conditions: The deed securing this
is called a further charge. If however, the outstanding debt is merged with the fresh advance
and a deed is executed to secure the repayment of the total debt, it is a fresh mortgage.

Duty on further charge is payable under Aiticle 28 while fresh mortgage is charge-
able under Art. 35. of Sch 1A.

Family Arrangement and family settlement:- Family settlement is'a kind of compromise.

‘When members of a family compromise their disputes about family property or the property of

arelation about the inheritance of which there is a boniafide dispute between them, the transac-

» tion is known as family settlement or family:arrangement.

General power and Special Power:- A special agency exists when there is an authority to
doa single act, a general agency exists when there is a delegation to do all acts connected with
a particular trade, business or employment.

“A person who is authorised to execute a particular deed or sign a particular con-
tract or to-purchase a particular property or merchandise is a special agent. Buta person who
is authorised to execute all deeds, sign all contracts or to purchase all goods required in a
particular business or employment is a general agent. The general power falls under Article
42(6), (d) and (g) while special power falls under Anticle 42(a) and (b) of Sch 1A.

Gift and Settlement:- Sce Gift (S.No, 32) and Settlement (S.No.66) of Chapter 2 on
Definitions. -

Hypothecation and Pawn or 1 Pledge: Hypothecation’ is mortgage of movable property without
possession. Pawn or pledge is mortgage of movable property with possession. The former is
chargeable‘ with Rs: 5/- if unattested and ‘under Article7 if attested. The latter is chargeable
under Article 7. of Sch. LA

Indemnity Bond and Security Bond:- Indemnity. Bond is a bond:by which‘one party prom-
ises to save the other from the loss caused.to him by the conduct:of the promisor or of any
other person. (Sec. 124 of Contract Act). It falls under Article 30.0f Sch. 1A whereas security
bond is one which is executed by way of security for due execution of an office or to account
for money ‘or property received by virtue thereof or execuited by a stirety to secure the due
performaiice of a contract. It falls under Article 48 of Sch. 1A.

Impounding and compounding:- See Chapter 2 on definition of “ “ Compounding” and “im-

. pounding” (S.No 19 and 33).

A deficitly stamped document is impounded and deficit stamp duty and {penalty are

not necessarily be impounded. The parties‘can-be prosecuted or the offence canbe condoned
(Compounded) on payment of composition fee.

Instrument and Document:- Every writing containing evidence or information is a docu-
ment while an instrument includes a document by which any right or liability is, or purports to
32.

be, created, transferred, limited, extended, extinguished or recorded (Sec. 2(14)). But in Stamp
Act, copies and affidavits which do not create etc., such rights are brought within the purview
of instruments.

Lease and usufructuary Mortgage:- In a lease of usufruct, there is a transfer of right to
enjoy the usufruct of the property in consideration of rent or umpsum amount. The amount
paid is not debt and no security is created in the property.

In an Usufructuary Mortgage the mortgagor delivers possession of the property
to the mortgagee and authorises him to appropriate the usufruct (profits) in satisfaction of the
debt and interest. A security is created in the property for the repayment of the debt. (Section
58(b) T-P.Act). Lease is chargeable under Article 31 while the Usufructuary Mortgage is
chargeable under Art. 35(a). of Sch. 1A.

Lease for indefinite period and Lease in perpetuity:- Where the period is not specified in
a lease, it is chargeable under Article 31(a)(viii) as a lease for indefinite period and where a
lease is granted permanently it is a perpetual lease chargeable under Art. 31(a)(vii). of Sch LA.

Lease for Money advanced and Mortgage with possession:- In both the cases, a lumpsum
is paid and possession of property is given to the person who paid the amount. In a lease there
is a transfer of right to enjoy the property in consideration of the amount paid whéreas in MWP
there is transfer of right in the property to secure the repayment of the money advanced. The
crucial test is creation of security over the property. If security is created it is a mortgage
otherwise it is a lease.

Sec. 2(16) & 2(17). Lease for money advanced is chargeable under Article 31{b)
and MWP under Art. 35(a). of Sch 1A.

Lease with advance and lease for premium:- Advance is repayable on.the termination of
lease Whereas premium is neither adjustable in rent nor. repayable. It is a price paid for the
grant of lease-a consideration. (Sec 105 T-P. Act).

Lease and Licence:- A licence differs from a lease. in that under a. lease.an interest in
immovable property is transferred while no interest is transferred under a licence. A licence is
unlike a lease, generally revocable and not assignable. It is revocable only under some special
circumstances (Sec 60 of Easement Act). Control and possession over the property is the main
criteria. If this is with the owner, it would be a mere licence, otherwise a lease. Licence is
chargeable under Art. 6 (Agreement) while lease is chargeable under Art .31. of Sch LA

Mortgage and Charge:- A mortgage is a transfer of an interest in specified property while in
a charge there is no such transfer but only the creation of a right of payment out of certain
property.(Sec. 58 and 100 of T.P. Act). For the purpose of Stamp Act both are chargeable as

- Mortgage.
272

38: MWP and usufructuary Mortgage:- Both are chargeable under Art..35(a) of Sch.:1A. In
MWP the mortgagee enjoys the property in lieu of interest and retransfers the property on
repayment of the mortgage money while in the usufructuary mortgage he enjoys the property
in lieu of principal and interest and retransfers it after the stipulated period.

Security Bond (Mortgage) under the said Article: (Vide also S. No. 64 of Chapter 2 - Defini-

42. Partition and Dissolution of Partnerships Partition isan’ instrument vinersby 1 the co-

the partners in which case it is to-be treated as dissolution of Partnership and partition charge-

43. Partition and family settlement:- When co-owners of property divide: it into severality, the
instrument recording itis called a partition deed (Sec. 2(15)). The duty is,payable.on.the value
of separated. share under Art.40. When the members. of a family compromise their disputes
about family property or the property inherited by them, it is a family settlement. The duty is
payable on the value of entire Property under Art. 49. A of Sch 1A.

Proviso (A) to Section 35 and Sec, 40(1)(B):- When an insufficiently stamped document
is produced before a Court in evidence, the Court shal! impound it and admit it in evidence only
if the deficit duty and penalty equal to ten times the deficit duty subject to a minimum of Rs.15/
= are paid (Vide Section 35, Provisio(a).)

When the Collector impounds a deficitly stamped document or receives such a
document, he shall levy the deficit duty and a penalty of Rs. 5/- or an amount not exceeding ten
times the deficit duty whether it exceeds or falls short of Rs.5/- (Section 40(1)(b).)

The Court has no option except to levy penalty equal to 10 times. A Collector has
discretion to levy a lesser penalty.

Prospecting Licence and Mining Lease:- See “Lease and Licence’(S.No 36 above). In a
prospecting licence, the licensee is authorised to prospect for minerals on a specified land. He
is not given exclusive possession of the land. He can enter it only for the specific purpose of
prospecting. Whereas in a mining lease, the lessee is given exclusive possession of the land to
excavate and take away the minerals on payment of Royalty or dead rent in addition to ground
rent and taxes etc. A prospecting licence is chargeable as an agreement under Article 6 while
mining lease is chargeable under Art. 31. of Sch 1A.

Pronote and Bill of Exchange:- Both are negotiable instruments. The broad distinction be-
tween the two is that in the former, the executant promises to pay himself, while in the latter, he
directs a third person to pay. Bill of Exchange falls under Article 13 and Pronote under Art. 49
of Sch-I (Both Central List).

Receipt and Release:- A receipt merely acknowledges the receipt of money in satisfaction
of debt etc., whereas Release is an instrument whereby a right or claim is relinquished. Eg. If
the Mortgagee merely acknowledges receipt of principal or interest or both it is a mere receipt,
whereas if words denoting release of mortgage right are used it becomes a Release charge-
able under Art. 46. of Sch 1A.

A receipt is chargeable under Article 53, Schedule — I (Central List) while Re-
lease is chargeable under Article 46, (Sch. I-A) (State List). However the stamp duty on
Release of mortgage right is limited to Rs.50/- on par with “Reconveyance of mortgaged
property.

Receipt and acknowledgement of Debt:- An acknowledgement of debt is one executed in
order to supply evidence of already existing debt while in contrast a receipt is executed in token
of acknowledgment of satisfaction of debt or. acknowledgement of receipt of money ad-
vanced. Acknowledgement of debt is chargeable under Art. 1 of Sch. 1-A (State List) while
Receipt is chargeable under Art, 53 Sch -I (Central List).

Rectification deed and Ratification deed:- A document by which a clerical error in a
previously executed instrument is rectified is a rectification deed requiring no stamp duty vide

Section 4 and Section 6 :- Sec. 4 deals with a single transaction being completed. through
several instruments i.c., supplemental deeds . The full duty is payable on the principal deed and
each supplemental deed is chargeable with a duty of Rs.5/-. This Section applies, to sales,
mortgages and settlements only.

Sec. 6.deals with an instrument coming under several descriptions in Schedule | or
1A as the case may be. The duty payable is the highest of the duties payable under each of the
descriptions. .

Section 5 and Section 6:- Section 5 relates to a document which contains several distinct
matters capable of being carried out through different documents. The distinct matters may be
of (a) same category or of () different categories. Eg.(a) Sale of different plots of lands to
different purchasers for separate considerations and by confirmation of oral gift and sale ex-
ecuted by donor and donee in favour of purchaser. The duty chargeable is the aggregate of the
duties chargeable on each matter.

Sec:6 deals with instruments containing a single matter but coming under several
description of the Schedule, eg., Sale with indemnity, Dissolution of partnership and partition of
the assets between the partners,. Trust and settlement etc., The duty chargeable is the highest
of the duties chargeable under each description.

Section 5 and Section 14:- Any instrument relating to several distinct matters shall be

_ chargeable with the aggregate of the duties chargeable on such distinct matters (Sec. 5).

61.

62.

Sec. 14 prohibits that no second instrument chargeable with duty shall be written
on stamped paper on which an instrument was already executed. Certain endorsements re-
ferred to in the proviso are exempted from this provision.

Sec 5. deals with more than one distinct matter contained in a document while
Sec, 14 prohibits drawing up another instrument on an already executed instrument.

Section 31 and Section 41:- Application under Sec. 31 is made to the Collector for the
determination of the Stamp duty payable on an instrument whether executed or not, when the
party is in doubt about the duty payable. It can be at any time but a certificate under Sec, 32
can be added only if the document is presented within one month from the date of execution in
India if executed or 3 months from date of receipt in India. Application under Sec. 41 can be
made to the Collector within one year from the date of execution. The application must satisfy
the Collector that the omission to stamp the document duly, was occasioned by accident, mis-
take or urgent necessity and he must offer to pay the proper duty or deficit as the case may be

Section 33 and Section 41:- Sec. 33 relates to deficitly stamped documents produced be-
fore court or.an officer in charge of a public office other than a police official in evidence or in
the discharge of its or his duties as the case may. be . The court or the officer shall impound
such document.

Sec. 41 deals with deficitly stamped documents voluntarily produced before the
Collector within one year from the date of execution with an offer to pay. the deficit duty.

Surrender of Lease and Transfer. of Lease: Surrender is yielding up of the right to a person,
who has the immediate estate in reversion. Surrender of unexpired portion of the tenancy right
in favour of the lessor is surrender of lease.chargeable under Art..52 of Sch 1A while transfer
of lease is the conveyance of lessees whole right in the lease to the assignee, a third party,
chargeable under Art. 54 of Sch. 1A.

Transfer of trust property from trustee to beneficiary and Release of benaini right:-
Release of benami right.by the benamidar in favour.of the real purchaser through a Court sale
and transfer of trust property if the original was a private sale. But after amendment of Art, 46,
both were ordered to be classified as:Release of benami right under Article 46B. Article 53(d)
applies when there were originally trust deeds and properties are transferred by trustees.

Transfer of lease and sublease. or underlease:- A sublease or underlease is a grant by a
lessee to another called underlessee: or sublessee of a part. or whole of his interest under the
original lease, reserving to himself a reversion. It falls under Art. 31 of Sch 1A.

A transfer of lease, on the other hand, conveys the lessee’s whole interest to the
assignee including the right to sue or be sued upon the covenants in the original lease. It falls
under Article 54 of Sch 1A.

Trust and Settlement:- For definition of the terms - Vide S.Nos 78 & 66 respectively of

* Chapter 2, on Definitions. A deed of trust is chargeable as a settlement if there is no prior

73.
74,

75.

76.

disposition of the property in favour of the beneficiary. Settlement is chargeable under Art. 49
while trust deed is chargeable under Article 55 of Sch 1A,

Under lease and assignment of lease:- See S.Nos. 71 & 69 above respectively.

Usufructuary Mortgage and Lease for Lumpsum paid in advance:- In both the cases
the entire amount is paid in advance. In usufructuary mortgage, the amount partakes the nature
of debt while in lease it is regarded as advance of rent. In former case there is security. over the
property for debt advanced. The former is chargeable under Article 35(a) and the latter under
Article 31(b) of Sch LA.

Will and Codicil:- Will is a legal declaration of the intention of the testator with respect to his
property which he desires to be carried into effect after his death. (Sec. 52(b) Indian Succession
Act.) Codicil isa supplement to the will intended to make modifications to the terms of the will.
Both are testamentary documents exempt from payment of Stamp Duty.

Will and Settlement:- Will is a testamentary document which comes into operation only after
the death of the testator and the last will alone prevails. The bequest can be in any body’s
favour and for any purpose. It requires no stamp duty.

Settlementisa non-testamentary disposition of property in consideration of marriage
or for distribution of property in favour of relatives or dependants or for religious or charitable
purpose (Sec.2 (24).) It should be stamped under Art. 49. Sch. I-A.

An instrument by which two brothers who are.members of Hindu Joint Family agree to
divide equally between them for a period of four years, the produce of their family lands
which are estimated to fetch Rs.5,000/- a year is chargeable as an agreement under Art.
6A of Sch. I-A (Note 11, P. 72, M.S.M Note 10,P.178, A.PS.M)

tope for a period of ten days in consideration of Rs. 500/- is chargeable as an agreement
under Article 6(A)(i) of Schedule I-A .

An instrument whereby the executant mortgages without possession immovable property
for Rs.562/- and agrees to perform the agricultural labour in lieu of interest for the
claimant for one year on payment of Rs. 200/- is a mortgage for Rs. 562 and an agreement
not otherwise provided for chargeable with higher duty under Art. 35(b) of Sch. I-A read
with Sec. 6 (Vide Note. 23 on P.{23, M.S.M, Note 20 on P.244, A.P.S.M).

An agreement to sell agricultural land for Rs.4,000 in which advance of Rs.1,000/-
was taken and the balance of Rs. 3,000/- is to be paid to a third party towards a loan and
possession of the land is given at the time of agreement, is chargeable with a duty only as
an agreement under Art. 6, Sch. I-A if there is a specific mention in the agreement for
execution of a separate sale deed at a future date. After the amendment to the Stamp Act
by Act 17/86 which came into force on 16-8-1986, Agreement to sell followed by posses-
sion of property is chargeable as a Sale (excluding T.D) and rebate will be given on the
sale deed. (Vide Explanation under Article 47-A of Schedule J-A.)

A prospecting licence granted by Government to an individual for carrying operations for
an annual prospecting fee of Rs. 210/- and security deposit of Rs. 400/-. Such document
should be stamped as an agreement under Art. 6, Sch I-A (Vide Note 24 under Art. 5, Sch.
I. (P.75 Stamp Manuai). The security deposit of cash need not be separately charged as
mortgage in view of the ruling of the Supreme Court in Forest lease case in which it was
held that money is not a specified property. (See 8, 13 and 15 above.)

In an instrument between forest department and a Contractor it was recited that in respect
of a bamboo coupe the contractor was to be allowed to fell and remove bamboos for a
period of 5 years for a rent of Rs. 5,000/- P.A. The contractor had deposited an ammount of
Rs. 5,000/- in cash as security for due performance of the terms and it was tobe adjusted
towards rent of last year. The document is only an agreement if the bamboos are in grown
up stage and that the period of 5 years is allowed to cut and remove them. Deposit of cash
need not be charged as mortgage as it is not a specified property. So the document is only
an agreement. (NB under item 19 of Page 180 of A.P.S.M.and Note 4 on Page 14, A.P.S.M.)
If they are in growing stage it amounts to a lease.

13. ‘B’ released his joint right in his family property worth Rs. 13,450/- in favour of C who has
no relationship with him and the fact of pre-existing right is not mentioned.

A Release can be only in favour of a person having pre-existing right in the property.
Release in favour of an outsider for no consideration becomes a Gift. The document is
therefore chargeable to a Stamp Duty under Article 29, Sch. J-A and attracts Transfer
Duty also.

Mortgage. With Possession

‘A’ executed a Mortgage deed without possession in favour of ‘B’ for Rs. 1,000/- Subsequently
the mortgagor gives a power authorising the mortgagee to collect the rents. The document
becomes a Mortgage with possession (vide explanation under Art. 35). Stamp duty already
paid on simple Mortgage can be deducted and the balance only to be paid.

The Mortgagor has agreed to pay a rent of Rs. 10 PM for occupying the house mortgaged and
also pay Rs. 10 P.M for 5 years to a chit fund company on behalf of the mortgagee towards
discharge of mortgage debt. Constructive possession is deemed to have been given to the
mortgagee: The duty is to be paid as a M.W.P for the amount of original mortgage.

In a Mortgage without possession for Rs.2000/- the executant authorises the mortgagee to
collect Rs.20/- every month from the lessee of the mortgagor towards interest. The transaction
becomes a M.W.P

‘A’ Mottgaged his properties to ‘B’ for Rs.3,000/- on 7-3-2004. On 10-3-2004 he executed a
power authorising the mortgagee to manage the mortgaged properties and appropri ‘iate profits
in lieu of interest. The transaction amounts to M.W.P (Vide explanation under Art. 35, M.W.P.)
for Rs. 3,000/- dt. 7-3-2004

A further charge with possession for Rs. 520/- on a simple mortgage for Rs. 700/-. The docu-
ment is to be treated as M.W.P: for Rupées 1,220 and rebate should be given on the stamp duty
paid on the original mortgage.

. Simple mortgage for Rs. 1,000/- executed in favour of general power of attorney holder in

respect of property in Hyderabad. According to explanation under Art. 35, Sch. I-A the docu-
ment should be treated as Mortgage with possession chargeable under Art. 35(a) besides
transfer.duty.

A deed of mortgage for Rs. 25,000/- executed by the Director of Agriculture, Hyderabad in
favour of President of India securing the repayment of loan obtained from Central Government
to purchase a house in Hyderabad. The document is exempt from payment of duty (vide item
No.27. Notification No.13, dated 17-12-1938. (P. 6 of A:P.S.M))

A deed of further charge in which the amount outstanding under the original mortgage and the
amount additionally secured have been consolidated and repayment of this amount is secured,
Stamp duty has to be levied on.the consolidated amount: However if the Supplementary mort-
gage deed merely contains the details of the original mortgage but secures the repayment of
only the additional amount, stamp duty to be levied is on the value of the such additional amount.
(1G’S No.S1/18671/93 dated 2-9-1993).

‘B’ the son gets a share in father’s property if the latter dies intestate. So he has pre-existing

Father relinquished his right in the ancestral as well as in the self acquired properties worth Rs.
29,000/- of equal value of A & B Schedules in favour of his son by taking ‘A’ Schedule and
relinquishing his right over ‘B’ Sch. Joint family properties.

The document can be classified as a Release if it is executed by the father alone and
if there are no other co-parceners to the property. The duty payable is under Article 46-A, Sch.
I-A. If there are other heirs or interested parties to the property, the document is to be treated.
as a conveyance and stamped accordingly.

Settlement / Release

On 5-6-2004 ‘A’ executed a deed valued Rs.30000 assigning certain iands to ‘B’ for his main-
tenance. On the same day “B’ executed a deed of Release without mentioning the amount of
consideration.

The first document is to be classified as ‘Settlement’ for Rs. 30,000/- chargeable with
a duty under Art. 49(A), Schedule.I-A. The second document is a Release of maintenance
right falling under Art. 46(A).

At the instance of elders, the husband, by. way of providing for maintenance, settles, immov-
able property valued Rs.6000/- without power of alienation on his first wife who is dumb. Cash
of Rs.,1,500/- and jewellery worth Rs.500/- are also given to her. The brother of the settlee has
to manage the property and after her death, the brother can enjoy the property with power of
alienation. . .

The document is to be treated as a Settlement for Rupees 8,000/- (6,000/- + 1500/- +
500/-) to wife and Gift for Rs. 6,000/- if the movables given to wife would not pass on to her
brother. (Vide Article 49-A and 29. of Sch. I-A. B.P.1401/Q/67, dt. 3-6-1967)

(a) Transfer of land worth Rs,.3,500/- by a son in favour of his widowed mother in compromise
of her suit for maintenance. The document is chargeable as a Settlement for Rupees 3,500/-
with a duty of Rs. 105/- under Article 49-A Schedule I-A (Vide note under Sec. 2(10) P. 6 of
A.P.S.M. )

(b) Settlement for religious purposes in respect of property worth Rs.1000/- . The duty on
settlements for religious purposes was reduced to one half ( Vide item 86, Notification No 13,
dated 17-12-1938.) It therefore works out to Rs.30 under Art. 49-A of Sch. I-A:

Transfer of principal and interest due under a Simple Mortgage amounting to Rs.21,000/- to
Lord Sri Venkateshwara of Tirupati in consideration of devotional love. Transfer of mortgage
to sons out of iove and affection was held as settlement in B.P. Misc. No. 1282, dt, 17-7-1944.
Following the same principle the document, being in favour of a deity has to be classified as a
settlement for religious purpose chargeable with a reduced duty of Rs.630/- under Art.49-A.
read with item 86 of Notification No.13, dt. 17-12-1938.

A Settlement for life in favour of a purohit in respect of immovable property worth Rs.1000/.
As the settlement is for life only it need not be classified as a Gift. It may be stamped as a
settlement with a duty of Rs.60/- under Art. 49-A of Sch. I-A.

Instrument in favour of Zilla Grandhalaya Samstha for charitable purpose value of property _
being Rs.8342/- .The duty payable is one half of the duty as a Settlement. Therefore it works
out to Rs.510 ,2 =Rs.255/-

Dociment in favour of would be wife for consideration of Rs.6926/-.According to the latest
order of Government the document is chargeable as a Settlement with a duty of Rs.420 under
Art. 49-A of Sch. LA.

CHAPTER=5'
RULES UNDER THE INDIAN STAMP ACT, 1899
CHAPTER IT
PRELIMINARY

Short title: These rules may: be:called the. Indian Stamp Rules, 1925.

Definitions In these tules, . .
a) “The Act” means the Indian Stamp Act, 1899 (II of 1899):
b). “Section” means’ a Section of the Act
¢) “Schedule” means a Schedule of the Act

d) The Inspector General of Stamps means the Inspector General of Stamps, Andhra
Pradesh, Hyderabad or any. officer appointed by the Government to perform the
functions or Inspector General of Stamps.

a

e) “Government” means, unless there is anything repugnant in the subject or context
the “Government of Andhra Pradesh”.

Description of Stamps:
(1) Except as otherwise provided by the Act or by these Rules —

(i) All duties with which any mstrument is.chargeable shall be paid and’such pay-
ment shall be indicated on such instrument, by means of stamps issued by Gov-
ernment, for the purposes of the Act.

(ii), A-stamp which by any word or ‘words on the face of it is appropriated to any
particular kind of instrument, shall not be used for an instrument of any other
kind.

(iit) Stamps purchased in A.P. State.alone shall be used’for instruments chargeable
with duty under the Act as in force in that State.

Provided that stamps. purchased in the State of A.P. may also be used. for
instruments chargeable with.duty under the Act as in.force in the transferred
territories.

Note: The term “Transferred Territories: shall mean the areas transferred to the
state of A-P under the provisions of A.P. and Madras (Alteration of Boundaries)
Act, 1959 (Central Act 56 of 1959).
320

(iv) ‘The stamp paper will bear the endorsements of the stamp-vendors prescribed
under:the rules made under Section:74. :

 

  

Q) There shall be two » Kinds of ‘stamps for indication ‘of the ‘payment of ‘duty ‘with which
instruments are chargeable, namely-

(a) Impressed stamps and

(b) : Adhesive stamps.

CHAPTER II
-- OF IMPRESSED STAMPS
4.. Hundi: ao

 

(1) ~ Hundis, other than hundis which tnay be stamped with an adhesive stamp under Section
_ IL, shall be written on paper as follows namely: coe

(a) A hundi payable otherwise than on demand, but not at more than one year after
, date of sight, and for an amount not, exceeding rupees thirty thousand in value,
shall be written on paper on which a stamp of the proper | value bearing the word
‘Hundi’ has been. engraved or embossed.

(b) A hundi for an amount exceeding rupees thirty thousand jn value, or payable at
more than one year after date of sight, shall be written on paper supplied forsale
-by the. Government. to. which a label has been affixed by Inspector General of
Stamps, and impressed by him in the manner prescribed by rule 11.

(2) Every sheet of paper on which a hundi is written shall be not less than 85/8 inches long and
5 8s inches wide atid no plain paper shail be subjoined thereto.”

(3) The provision of sub-rule qa) of rule 7 shall apply i in the c: case of. Hundis.

5. Promissory Notes and Bill of Exchange: A Promissory note, or Bill.of Exchange shall,
except as provided by Section 11 or by rules 13 and 17, be written on paper.on which stamp of
the proper value, with or without the word “Hundi’ has been engraved or embossed.

6. Other instruments:

(1) Bvery other insttument chargeable with duty shall; excépt'as' provided by Section 11 or by
roles 10,12,:13 be written on paper on Which a stamp of the proper value, not bearing the
Word “Hundi” has been engraved or embossed.
321

(2) A stamped paper, on which the stamp has been engraved or embossed purchased by or for
the use of a person shall be used only; by that person or his legal representative or duly
authorised agent of such person provided that the Govt. may relax the provision of this sub-
tule in genuine and deserving cases. : :

Provision where single sheet of paper is insufficient:

(1) Where two or more sheets of paper on which stamps are engraved or embossed are used
to make up the amount of duty chargeable in respect of any instrument, a portion of such
instrument shall be written on each sheet so used.

Note:-

i) The purchase of stamp papers for‘use together in one transaction does not operate
to prevent their being used separately for different transactions. Nor is there any
objection to the use of stamp papers purchased in one year together with other
stamp papers to be purchased subsequently.

i) The signature of executants and attesting witnesses do not form an integral portion
of.a deed. .

(2) Where a single sheet of paper, not being paper bearing an impressed hundi-stamp, is
insufficient to admit of the entire instrument being written on the side of the paper which
bears.the stamp, so much plain paper may be subjoined thereto as may be necessary for
the complete writing of such instrument.

PROVIDED that, in every such case a substantial part of the instrument shall be written on the
sheet which bears the stamp before any part is written on the plain paper'subjoined.

Note: y

(1) ' The Madras High Court has held that this sub rule (2) while favouring the writing
ofan instrument only on the side which bears the stamp, does not prohibit writing
on the other side.

(2). A Collector ruled that a Conveyance was not duly stamped the writing being on
’ both sides of the paper. The High Court held that, the ruling was wrong and that
both sides may be written upon.

(3). The words ‘substantial part’ apply not to length or quantity but to materiality. In
this view the price or rent, the names of the parties and the date which are set out
in the introductory portion of vernacular documents.are all material parts of the
documents. The date will only appear in this introductory portion:and in some
classes of vernacular documents it is the same with the price. Such introductory
322

portion of a vérnacular doctiment is a substantial part of the instrument within the
meaning of rule7. (The Advocate-General’s opinion in Board’s Proceedings No. 16/
+ 136, Mis: dated 17": January 1901)" aa 3

11. Mode of affixing and impressing, labels: bogus

(a) .The proper officer shall, upon any instrument specified in rule 10 being brought fo him
:.-before it is executed, and upon application being made to. him, affix thereto a label or
jabels or frank a stamp or stamps of such value as the applicant may require and pay for,
and impress or perforate’such label’ by means of a stamping’ machine or a perforating
machine or franking machine and also stamp or write on the face of the label or labels
the date of impressing or perforating the, same. In the case of instruments written on
parchment, the labels shall be further secured by means of metallic eyelets.

323

(b) On affixing any label or labels under this rule, the Proper officer shall, where the duty
amounts to rupees five or upwards, write on the face of the label or labels his initials and
where the duty amounts to rupees twenty or upwards , shall also attach his usual signa-
ture to the instruments immediately under the label or labels.

(c) Any principal assistant of the Proper officer may discharge the functions of the Proper
officer under sub-rule (2) if empowered by the Government in this behalf.

12. Certain instruments to be stamped with Impressed labels:

(1) Instruments executed out of the State and requiring to be stamped after their receipt in
the State (Other than instruments which, under Section 11 or Rule 13, may be stamped
_ with adhesive Stamps) shall be stamped with Impressed labels.

(2) Where any such instrument as aforesaid is taken to the Collector under Section 18, sub-
section (2), the Collector, unless he is himself the Proper officer, shall send the instru-
ment to the Proper officer, remitting the amount of duty paid in respect thereof; and the
Proper officer shall stamp the instrument in the manner prescribed in rule i1 and return
it to the Collector for the delivery to the person by whom it was produced.

CHAPTER IV
MISCELLANEOUS

18. Provision for cases in which improper description of stamp is used: When an instru-
ment bears a stamp. of proper amount, but of improper description, the Collector may, on the
payment of the duty with which the instrument is chargeable, certify by an endorsement that it
is duly stamped.’ ue . . . .

PROVIDED THAT, where the stamp borne on the instrument is a Postage stamp. and the
proper description’ of stamp is a stamp bearing the words ‘Indian Revenue’ the Collector shali
so certify if the instrument was executed before, and shall not so certify if it was executed on
or after the 1* April 1935.

19. Evidence as to circumstance of claim to refund or renewal: The Collector may require
any person claiming a refund or renewal under Chapter V of the Act; or-his duly authorised
agent, to make an oral‘deposition on oath or affirmation, or to file an-affidavit; setting forth the
circumstances undet which the claini has arisen and may also, if he thinks fit, call :for the
evidence of witnesses in support of the statement setforth in any:such deposition or affidavit.

19A. Furnishing of affidavits regarding instruments executed out of India? The Collector,
may require any person or his duly authorised agent presenting an instrument executed out of
India for stamping under Section 18 or for opinion under section 31 to furnish an affidavit or
other evidence as to the date of its first receipt in India.

20. Payment of allowance in respect of spoiled or misused stamps or on the renewal of
Debentures : When an application is made for the payment, under Chapter V of the Act, of
an allowance in respect of a stamp which has been spoiled or misused or for which the appli-
cant has no immediate use, or on the renewal of a debenture, and an order is passed by the
Coliector sanctioning the allowance or calling for further evidence in support of the application,
then, if the arnount of the allowance of the stamp given in lieu thereof is not taken or if the
further evidence required is not furnished, as the case may be, by the applicant within one year
of the date of such order, the application shall be struck off and the spoiled or misused stamps
326

Gif any) sent to the Inspector General of Stamps or other officer appointed in this behalf by the
State Government for destruction.

21. Mode of canceling original Debenture or refund under Section $5: When the Collector

makes'a refund under Section 55, he shall cancel the original Debentire by. writing on or across
it the word ‘Cancelled’ and his usual signature with the date thereof,

22. Rewards: On the conviction of any offender under the Act, the Collector: may.grant to any

person who appears to-him to have contributed thereto a reward not exceeding such.sum as
the State Government may fix in this behalf.

lL. _ Short, title and commencement — (1) These rules may..be called.the Andhra Pradesh Stamp
(Prevention of ‘underyaluation of. Instruments) Rules,.1975,

 

 

(c) “Form” " means a form appended to these rules;

@ “Registering officer means the registering officer appointed under the Indian Reg-
“ tion Act, 1908 (Central Act RV of 1908), :

    
  

 

(e) “Section” means a section of the Act

3. Furnishing of statement of market value:~ (1) Ifan-instrument:related:to a‘aumber of items of
Proj erty, the m market value shall be specified i in respect of each item separately. For this pur-

 

: planation: 4:- If.an instrument:covers:Jands; comprising several “survey numbers or sub-
division-numbers;.the.market value shall:be specified:in-respect of the land covered by each
survey number or sub-division number, as the case may be'séparately:':

.. Explanation. 2:- If.an instrument telates.to only-one item of property ‘and:that property is a
building, or if an instrament related-to more than. one iter of property. and one or more such
331

items is or are a building or buildings , the particulars mentioned in the Annexure to these rules
shail be furnished in respect of the building or each of such buildings as the case may be.

(1) The registering officer shall, before registering an instrument, satisfy himself that
the party has enclosed to the instrument a statement, giving the market value in
respect of each of the properties.separately, as required by sub-rule (1) and Ex-
planations 1 and 2 above.

(2) The registering officer may for the purpose of satisfying himself whether the
market value or the consideration has been correctly furnished in the instrument
or not; make such enquiries, as he may deem fit. He may elicit from the parties
concerned any information haying a bearing on the subject and call for and exam-
ine any records kept with any Public Officer or authority.

(3) If the Registering Officer is of the opinion that the market value of the property
affected by the instrument is not correctly furnished, he shall keep the document
pending and without delay. refer the matter to the Collector with details of his
assessment of the Market Value arrived at by him in the Form. I. No copy of such
document shall be granted notwithstanding anything contained in any of the provi-
sions of any other Act or Rules. :

(4) The Registering officer shall maintain a register of such references to the Collec-

10. Procedure for the disposal of appeals:- (1) If an appéal is admitted by the appellate authority a
date shall be fixed for hearing the appeal. The appellate authority shal! issue’a notice to the
appellant informing him of the date on which and the time and place at which the appeal shall
be heard. Such notice shall also state that if the appellant does not appear on the day so fixed
or any other day to. which the hearing may be adjourned. The appeal shall be liable to be
dismissed for default or disposed of on merits ex-party.

(2) The appellate authority shall send a copy of the notice to the Collector together with copy
of the appeal and obtain the records of the case form the Collector.

11. Hearing of the appeal:- On the date fixed or on any other date to which the case may be
adjourned, the appellate authority shall hear the appellant and receive any evidence adduced on
his behalf. It shall also hear the person, if any, appearing on behalf of the Collector and receive
the evidence, if any adduced in support of the Collector’s order. ‘

12. Order in appeal: - (1) After considering all the evidence adduced and representations made on
behalf of the appeilant and the Collector ‘and examining the records of the case, the appellate
336

authority shall decide whether or not the market value. of the’ properties or the consideration
thereof, as determined by the Collector under sub-section Q) or. sub-section (3) of section
is correct, In case, the appellate ‘authority does not accept the valuation of the properties
“or the consideration determined by the Collector it shall determine the correct market value of
the properties or the consideration and the duty payable on the instrument. The appellate au-
thority shall embody its decision and the reasons thereof in an order.and communicate it to the
appellant, the Collector and the registering officer concerned.

 

Q) After receipt of the orders issued by the Appellate authority under Sub-Section (4) of
“Section 47-A: if the parties fail to pay deficit stamp ‘duty if “any, or if such deficit stamp
“duty is not recovered by coercive process uhder section 48, the Registering Officer
shall destroy the document after 5 years froin thé date of the orders of the Collector
under Sub-Section (2).of Section 47-A .

(3) Ifany officer by conducting spot inspection or otherwise detects that a document is under-
valued and reports to the Collector with his assessment, of. the value of: the property
affected by the docutnent within 2 years from the date of registration of such document
the Collector shall exercise his powers under sub-section (3) of séction 47-A

13. Return of records to Collector:- As soon as may be after the order is passed under rule 12, the
appellate authority shall return to the Collector the records obtained from him.

14. Rules of procedure: ~ (1) The appellate authority may adjourn the hearing of the appeal from
time to time, as it thinks fit’ ~~

(2) The appellate authority may at any ‘stage of the hearing of the appeal, call for any
. information, record or other evidenge from the Appellant or the Collector.

(3) In the appeal, the appellant may.appear.¢ either: in.person.or-through an advocate, or an
authorised agent.

. Procedure, :1908. (Act: Moot: 1908); .relating.to. the procedure to be followed. by. the

+ appellate authority in appeals against the order of the Civil Court shall, as far as may
°be, apply to appeals under sub- section (4) of “section 41. AY

‘CHAPTER -7
ADJUDICATION UNDER SEC. 41 OF THE STAMP ACT IN RESPECT OF
DOCUMENTS FALLING U/Sec. 47-A

In cases where a document is not duly stamped, then Collector has power under Section
41 to adjudicate the document if satisfied that the omission to duly stamp has been occasioned by
accident, mistake or urgent necessity. The deficit duty can be collected by the Collector if the
person voluntarily produced the document offering to pay the deficit duty. There is no provision to
collect penalty under this section. The time limit for the party to avail the benefit of this section is
one year from the date of execution. All District Registrars and Sub-Registrars are Collectors
under this section. If a document is insufficiently stamped, the Sub Registrar has to advise the
party to take the advantage conferred under Section 41 before the expiry of one year from the date
of execution of the document.

* Documents executed not in conformity with the market value, the ordinary recourse is to
refer such cases under section 47-A to the authorities empowered to determine Market Value. If
the party pleads that by mistake he had not adopted the market value and expresses his willingness
to pay the deficit duty, he may be allowed to do so and instead of following the procedure in section
47-A the registering officer shall follow the procedure laid down in sec. 41.

Where the party voluntarily seeks to get the document adjudicated in case where he fails to
pay duty on market value, the party shall file an application duly affixed with Court-fee label of
Re. 1/- under Sec. 41 for adjudication volunteering to pay the deficit stamp duty. [f the application
is filed before registration, the deficit stamp duty and the registration fee should be levied on the
Market Value. The Sub Registrar should file the application with his remarks. The deficit duty
should be credited to relevant account head.

Certificate under Sec, 41 on the document shall be added in the following form after
tetaining an office copy of the certificate in the file.

CHAPTER-8

IMPOUNDING OF DOCUMENTS

1. Every person having by law or consent of parties authority to receive evidence and every
person in charge of a Public office except an officer of Police, before whom any instrument
chargeable in his opinion, with duty is produced or comes in the performance of his functions shall,

if it appears to him that such Instrument is not duty stamped, impound the same as per Section
ee ecenenannineent ane ee AAT I
33(1) of Indian Stamp Act. °
TTT

2. The expression “Duly Stamped” in Section 2(11) would seem to mean not only bearing
stamp of proper value but also stamped at the right time (Sec. 17. 18 and 19} in the proper manner
(Sec. 13 and 14) with proper description of stamp (Sec. 10 and 11) under the Stamp Rules and duly
cancelled (Sec. 12 and 13)

3..A Sub Registrar.is an officer in charge of Public office within the Section 33.

4, When irisufficiently stamped document is presented before a Registering Officer, he shall
impound it under Sec.33.of Indian Stamp Act and forward it to-the Collector (Dist. Registrar)
registration being suspended (R.R.32 (ii) and pending orders on.such reference toa Registrar or
prior to sending a document to the Collector, the registering officer may however record admission
of the execution and examinatiori-of witnesses if any(R.R.32(iii)).

5. In every Registration Office a Register (Form V- Vide Proforma after para 6 below) shall
be maintained in which every document impounded shall be ertered on the date on which the
registering officer decides to impound the same [S.0.462].

6. A document impounded by a Sub Registrar shail be forwarded to the Registrar to whom
he is a subordinate together with an extract of columns { to 8 of the Register of impounded
documents signed and dated by the Registering officer. A copy of the document shall also accom-
pany the extract. No covering letter is necessary [S.0.462}.

8. (i) The Registrar, if he is of the opinion that a document forwarded to him is not duly
stamped, shall inform the Sub-Registrar concerned of the deficit stamp duty and penalty to be
collected (S.0.462) after giving reasonable. opportunity to the party to represent his case before

“final orders are passed (Bds.LDis 683/W/64. dt. 20-02-1965 with IG’s Endt. No. 51/5768/65-1
Dt. 12-3-1965)

(i) On receipt of this intimation the Sub-Registrar shall communicate the decision to both
the claimant and the executant requiring payment to him by either of them of the amount (S.0.462).

(iti) The amount shall be brought to Account °C” and remitted into Treasury on a separate
challan.

(iv) The (Miscellaneous) receipt shall be granted for the amount received.

(v) The fact of payment of deficit duty and penalty. shall be reported immediately to the
Registrar with particulars of name and residence of payer (Sec. 42(I) of I.S.Act) and date of
payment and challan to be submitted to Registrar.

(vi) On receipt from the Sub-Registrar of the intimation of payment the Registrar shal!
endorse the document as required by Sec. 42(1) of Indian Stamp Act and return it to Sub-Registrar.

(vii) On receipt of the document, duly certified by the Registrar, the Sub-Registrar will
admit the document to Registration.

(viii) The entire stamp duty paid on the document i.c., duty paid in the shape of stamp
initially and duty paid in cash subsequently should be accounted for in Account ‘A’ and Transfer
Duty should be allocated on the entire stamp duty. If the document is found to be insufficiently
stamped at the time of presentation some parties come forward with new stamps purchased to
make up the deficiency and attempt to attach them to the document by cancelling them with a line
or by some writings. This is highly objectionable and Stamp. Act does not allow such a practice.
Documents produced in connection with the Undervaluation enquiry, the Sub-Registrar cannot
impound as the enquiry is an administrative function but he can send it to Registrar for impounding
if it is not properly stamped. After impounding under Sec. 33 the Registrar can proceed under
Secs. 40 and 42 of Indian Stamp At but not under Secs. 35 and 38(1) as it is not admitted in
evidence. The person who produced deficitly stamped document has to pay the deficit stamp duty.

9. A Sub Registrar cannot impound a registered document since he becomes functus
officio after he registers the document (Inspector’s General $1/27106/68 dated 28-10-65) (AIR
1966 Madhya Pradesh 20(V) 58 CM R.G. 1967 Dec. 1) A copy of document duly executed and
344

presented by the party to the Sub-Registrar as: copy for official use has to be impounded if it does
not bear stamp duty as required under art 21 of Sch 1A (.0's.No.S3/23707/69, dt 17-06-1970).

 

10. If an unstamped ¢ or deficit! stamped document is presented i in evidence in the course,
of will enquiries, the Sub- Registrar can décumént under section 35 and add-certificate
under section 42(1) of Indian Stamp Act after collecting. deficit stanip duty and penalty. If a wrong
adjudication is made by a Registrar as Collector and the document is returned to the Sub-Registrar,
the Sub-1 Registrar has no option except to register it: Sub-Registrar should clearly understand the
basis of impounding, If on the face of it the document is duly stamped, it has to’be accepted. At
times SubRegistrars ‘impound documents which should be: dealtwith under Sections 27.or.47-A
-Following illustrations will clarify the point. ces ble olen gi

Instructions On Allocation & Adjustment of ‘Transfer Duty
i. G. Oo. Ms. No. 725, PR & R.D (P&S) Dept., dt. 16-12-88.
Notification

In exercise of powers conferred by sub-section (3) and (4) of section 73 of Andhra
Pradesh Gram Panchayats Act, 1964 (Andhra Pradesh Act 2 of 1964) the Governor of Andhra
Pradesh hereby makes the following amendments to the Andhra Pradesh Gram Panchayats
(Duty on Transfer of Property) Rules, 1965 issued to G.O.Ms.No. 788, Panchayat Raj (Pts.
VHI) Department dated 8 July, 1965 and published in Rules Supplement to Part VII of the Andhra
Pradesh Gazette, dated the 29" July 1965:-

Amendments
In the said rules (1)
(i) For sub-rules (1) (c), the following sub rule shall be substituted, namely:-

(a) The said account shal! be consolidated every month, for each Gram Panchayat by
the Sub-Registrar”.

(b) Inspector General of Registration and Stamps, Andhra Pradesh, Hyderabad shall
issue executive instructions regarding the maintenance of accounts by Registering
Officer in this behalf.”

(ii) For sub-rufe(2) the following sub-rule shall be substituted, namely:-

(1) In the case of instruments which do not come into the hands of the Registering
officers, the Collector, before whom any such instrument is produced under sec-
tion 31 or 41 of the Indian Stamp Act, or who impounds any such instrument
under Section 33 of the said Act or received, a certificate of the duty and penalty
paid in respect of any such instrument or the original of any such instrument under
section 38 of the Act shall keep an account of the duty paid in respect of each
such instrument showing separately the duty imposed by the Stamp Act and the
Transfer duty and forward it to the Sub-Registrar concerned within ten days after
the close of every month, a monthly consolidated account with particulars in the
case of each such instrument regarding the names of executant and claimant., the
date of execution, the nature of the transaction, the amount of consideration, the

CHAPTER =11-A
-Undervaluation Of Documents

Undervaluation of deeds with intent to defraud the Government is an offence under See. 64
of the Stamp Act. Sec.27 lays down that all circumstances affecting the chargeability of an instrument
shall be fully and truly setforth in the deed. Contravention of the section is punishable with a fine
which may extend to Rs, 5,000/- ection 64) in-addition to the loss, caused on acet count of
undervaluation (Section 64-A) :

 

2 (a) When‘a Sub Registrar is credibly informed that an offence of undervaluation is committed
by a person, he may conduct an enquiry under S.0.482 and send a repot to the Registrar. If the
Sub-Registrar feels that there is sufficient evidence to make out a case, he. may in the-first instance
inform the concerned party that undervaluation is an offence under the Stamp Act and if he chooses
he may approach the Registrar for compounding the offence. (Sub Registrar Gr. is authorised to
compound offence (Vide G.O.Ms.No.494, Rev., Dt.(27-5-67). Ifthe party does not come forward
to get the offence compounded, the SubRegistrar may seek ordérs from the Registrar to sanction
prosecution. Ifthe party-comes forward to gét thé offence compounded hemay be given an
opportunity to do so: A letter of consent has to be obtained from the party. Thearnount of cormpounding
fee agreed to be paid shall be noted’ in-consent etter (1.G's:Procgs.No:S2/17154/A/714,
dt. 23-6-72).A note of compounding orders shall be made on the original document if available. The
noté in the register book should bé made in the language of the document at the foot of the-entry or
on the fly-leaf with necessary cross reference and in'red ink in Index 'entry itt the form prescribed
in'IG’s Procgs.NoG1/24403/A-61, dated: 1-12-1963. The note should be‘entered in Bncutnbrinee
Certificate also in the following form. . My 4 BF EEE

 

“The value of the property/ the consideration i 18 RS.cceeeseeteceeeeee sien a8 | determined by
the Distiict Registrar in his order No. . “a
compounding fee of Rs

: (Sub) Registrar. _

 

(b) A similar note shall be added in'cases where a Court holds the parties: +s guilty under
Section 27 and section 64 mentioning the :case number and-the ‘name’ of the ‘court. Note:of
undervaluation should be made in account ‘A’ also. (Vide Procgs. No, G3/9516/71, dated 31-12-
1971 of the 1.G.R&S). The Inspector General has ordered that compounding orders shall not be
passed till the entire compounding fee is paid by. the party..
359

3. Although the maximum fine that can be imposed by a court as a punishment for
undervaluation is Rs.5000/-, it does not mean that the compounding fee cannot be levied in excess
of Rs.5000/-. Normally compounding fee is computed taking into consideration the loss incurred by
the Government. (Section 64 A of the Stamp Act).

4, Sf during the course of an enquiry under S.0.482 an insufficiently stamped documents is
produced by a party or a witness, the Sub-Registrar cannot impound it. He can send it to the
District Registrar for impounding it as he deals with undervaluation cases ina statutory capacity as
Collector under Section 70. After impounding it, the Registrar may proceed under Section 40 and
42(4) sitice the document ‘is not_admitted in evidence within the scope of proviso (a) to Sec.35 of

- Stamp Act. Enquiry undet $.0.482 is only an administrative function and is not a statutory duty.
The person who produced the document has to pay the duty, (Vide Procgs. No.S2/36395/66),
dated 3-3-1967 of I.G.R &S).

5 (a) After the.advent of the Market Value Scheme, the procedure prescribed under $.0.482
required some reorientation.

 

(b) In cases where the party declines to get the offence compounded, filing of a complaint
before the Magistrate has to be resorted to. If the subject matter of the document is a Building the
Sub Registrar has to go to the Building and measure the same, observe its location ar location and all other

ane measure me Sar
factors and record. his observations. bly in the presence of the concerned pa parties and it
would also be advisable to obtain the signature of two independent mediators. He has to compare

‘The data with the one furnished in annexure IA signed by the executant and the claimant and filed
with the registering officer at the time of registration of the document. If there is variation resulting
in loss to the government a case of undervaluation can be made out. If the Sub Registrar has
information that the consideration is more than what is stated, he should examine the parties and
obtain unimpeachable evidence to prove the offence. He should send a comprehensive report to
the Registrar stating all the facts and circumstances expeditiously with copies of all documents and
should seek orders sanctioning prosecution. The Registrar may make a further enquiry if he considers
necessary and obtain any additional information, District Registrars are empowered to summon
any person to appear before them and to produce any document or thing in the possession or
control of such person. The powers are vested with District Registrars under Section 2 of the A.P,
of i Revenue Enquiries Act (Vide G.O.Ms.No.109, Revenue Department, dated 31-1-1967).

 

 

 

 

(c) The District Registrar after satisfying himself that a clear case of undervaluation is
ay made out may sanction prosecution in the proforma prescribed in S.0.487, as amended in 1.G’s

ee No.G3/17288/60, Dt: 25-8-61 (Vide Annexure)

in the mattér Under Sec 82(a) of the Registration Act., whoever intentionally makes any false
statement, whether on oath or not, and whether it is recorded or not before any officer acting in

execution of this act in any proceeding or enquiry under this act or abets anything made is punishable

under the Act with imprisonment for a term which may extend to seven years or with fine or both.

provisions of Sec.468 Criminal Procedure Code shall also be considered to judge the period of
limitation for filing a complaint, If necessary an application to condone the delay may also be filed

(e) If during the course of preliminary enquiry under $.0.482 it is disclosed that certain
persons other than the executant or claimant also are involved in,the commission of offence they
too may be added as accused, being abettors to the offence.

: CHAPTER-11-B
Rules Under Section 27 of the Indian Stamp Act 1899.
(G.O. Ms: No. 34 Revenue (Registration-I) Department dated 08-01-1999)
an : ee re 2
1. Short Title :=:"

These Rules may be called the “Andhra Pradesh Stamp (Inspection of Properties}
Rules, 19982”: . : oo ; :

2. Inspection of Property:- ‘The Inspector General of Registration and Stamps may by order,
authorise any officer under his control to conduct inspection of any property or’a class or category
of properties registered under the provisions of the Registration Act, 1908 for the purpose of
ensuring compliance with the provisions-of section 27 of the said Act and to assess the value of
such property.

 

3. Assessment of Property:- The officer authorised shall.complete the inspections entrusted to
him within such period as may be specified and assess the value of the properties with reference to
the market value prevailing and the construction rates prescribed in accordance with the Andhra
Pradesh Revision of Market Value Guidelines Rules, 1998. :

4, Action to be.taken due to wrong assessment and suppression of facts:- If the authorised
officer detects any deficit duty payable in respect of any instrument: :

_ .; (a) due to wrong assessment, cither the value or the duty payable, by the Registrering Officer,
che shall deal with such cases under the provisions of section 41-A of the Indian Stamp Act, 1899.

‘2 (6) due to suppression of facts affecting the chargeability of the instrument, the’authorised
officer ‘shall:issue-a notice-in such-form:as may be«prescribéd to-the. person concerned: within
fifteen (15) ‘days from the.date of inspection ‘giving.an opportunity to explain the reasons for the

_Yariation, . . weve died lets :

5. Compounding of Offence: '(1) If the person t6 whom a ‘notice has’ been issued under rule
A(b); gives a Consent in writing to get the offence comipounded, the authoriséd officer shall levy and
Collect compounding fee which shall in no case be less than the loss of revenue involved within
three months from the date of inspection and make necessary endorsement on the original instrument
in such form as may bé prescribed. The authorised officer shall forward .a copy ofthe proceeding

determining the value of the property and levying compounding fee for making necessary‘entries in
the.relevant registers and-recards, - - .

QyIf the ¢ompounding fee levied under sub rule 5(1) is not paid within the stipulated period,
it shall be recovered under the provisions of section 48 of thé Indian Stamp Act, 1899, :

22) Jf the person to whom a-notice.has:been issued under rule a does not respond or agree
to sheassesSre as i value ‘and the.deficit amount determmed by the authorised officer, the
authorised _officer shall.launch prosecution against the person under the provisions of section 64

based on the facts and circumstances as required under section 27 of the Indian Stamp Act, 1899.
at Aaroniero manseantts | ahaa EAA

6 Inspection of property already inspected by the authorised officer:- The Inspector General
of Registration and Stamps, for sufficient reasons to be recorded inwriting may order for inspection
of any property already inspected by any of the authorised officers and take appropriate action.

Sub Registrar’s Functions As Collector.
Under Indian Stamp Act,1899
1) Denoting duty already paid (Section 16):-

Section 16 of the Stamp Act provides for denoting upon certain documents the duty
already paid on the relevant original documents. All registering officers are “COLLECTORS” u/s
16 of the Indian Stamp Act 1899 (S.0.476(a)). A certificate under Section 16 denoting: the.duty
already paid on the relevant original instrument shall.be added on the subsequent instruments in
respect of which the duty was paid on the basis of the Stamp duty paid on the original instruments
(8.0.476(c)(i)). a

4

2) Documents for which Section 16 is applicable:-
The following categories of instruments attract Section 16 of the Stamp Act.

i) Sale in favour of mortgagee stamped under the last proviso to section
24 of the Stamp Act.

ii) Further charge with possession on Simple Mortgage
iii): Supplemental deed falling under section 4,

iv) Duplicates

v) Counterparts

vi) Lease deeds executed in favour of mortgagee without possession fall-
ing under the explanation to Article 35 of Schedule 1-A

vii) Auxiliary, collateral, additional and substituted Security deeds

viii) Lease, Partition:and:Settlement:deed: stamped.under Articles 31,40 &
49 respectively of Schedule 1A

_ Ax) Sale deeds executed. in pursuance. of @ an Agreement to > sell I stamped
either under Article or under Articl ATA of, Schedule LA

In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of Section 9 of the Indian Stamp Act,
1899 (Act. 11 of 1899) ‘and in supersession of ail previous notifications issued: from time to time
under the said clause of the said section, in so far as they relate to the State‘of Andhra Pradesh
(hereinafter referred to as the said Province) except, the Notification of the Government of India in
the Finance Department (Central Revenues) No.6, stamps, dated the 146 August, 1937, as subse-
quently amended, the Government of Andhra Pradesh : are hereby pleased to reduce to the extent
setforth in each case, the duties chargeable in the said Province under the said Act in respect of the
instruments hereinafter described under Nos. 30, 34,35,47,49, 51, 59, 72, 85 and 86 and to remit
the duties so chargeable in respect of instruments | of th other classes hereinafter described. (Pre-
amble amended by G.O.Ms.No. 506 Rey.dt 3-4. 1962 ).

CHAPTER - 16

Guidelines On System Of Payment Of Stamp Duty, Transfer Duty,

2)

3)

4)

3)

6)

Registration Charges Etc., Through Banks
(Vide Memo No. G1/16619/03 dated 27-10-2003 of the C & IGR&S Hyderabad).

The Government accorded permission to the Inspector General of Regn.& Stamps
to work out operational modalities for payment of money towards Stamp duty. Registration
Fee etc., into Government Treasury through the designated Branches of State Bank of India /
Hyderabad / Nationalized Banks (Vide G.O.Ms.No.953 dated 10-9-03 and G.O.Ms.No.958,
dated 13-9-2003.)

Accordingly, basing on the experience of implementation, this system in Hyderabad
District from 22-09-2003 the following operational Guidelines for collection of Stamp Duty,
Registration Fee etc., through designated Branches of State Bank of Hyderabad / State Bank
of India are issued.

Each Sub-Registrar office will have a separate designated Branch of State Bank of Hyderabad
/ State Bank of India. The name and address of that Branch should conspicuously be displayed
in the notice board of that SRO.

A non-drawal “Stamp Duty Collection Account” will be opened in the designated Branch of
the Bank as ordered in the G.Os mentioned above. Specimen signatures of the Bank authori-
ties will be furnished to the Sub-Registrars for verification of receipts issued by the Bank and
those of Sub-Registrars to the Bank authorities.

Two types of challan forms, ie., one challan for public use and another for SRO use are
designed. The District Registrars should get the challan forms printed in triplicate in light blue
colour estimating the requirement of the challans for the period upto March, 2004, The expen-
diture for printing may be met from user charges and the amount required for this purpose may
be got alloted by the Inspectore General of Registration & Stamps separately. Sufficient stock
of the challan forms and rubber stamps denoting the name of Bank Branch with A/C No.
should be made available both at the Banks and at the Sub-Registrar offices. The challans
shall be supplied free of cost to the parties.

The party has to pay the amount through challan (in triplicate) in the designated Branch of the
Bank, which is attached to that Sub-Registrar office only, where the document is to be pre-
sented and the Bank will give unique number to the challan and the receipt. The challan and
the receipt have to be accepted in that particular Sub-Registrar office only.

The Sub-Registrar/Assistant has to guide the public in filling up of the challan and add his
initials, ifthe party approaches him with challan before remittance. However, the parties are at
liberty to pay the amounts through challans directly in the designated Branches.

It is important to note that the payment of Stamp Duty, Transfer Duty, Registration fee etc.,
should be through a separate challan for each document by the parties concerned. Payment of

amounts through one challan for multiple documents should not be accepted as the challan and
receipt are unique for each document. ‘
400

» The Sub-Registrar shall, on production of the Challan and Bank receipt, verify the signature on
the receipt with the specimen signature of the’ Bank ‘authority and issue a receipt to the parties
with details of payment and procéed with registration if the document is otherwise in order. He

‘shall ote the details of payment made through Bank challan in the presentation endotsement
by denoting the amount through Bank challan/Receipt: After registration of the document the
following endorsement shall be-made on the page'containing registration endorsement”

 

‘Rs, towards Stamp duty including ‘Transfer duty. US4I1 ‘of 1. S.Act
and Rs. _ towards Registration fee on the chargeable value of
Rs. Z weré paid’ by the Party through SBI/SBH. Receipt Num:

Ber, dated: at Branch.”

8) Ifthe party desires to pay the Stamp duty, Transfer duty and Regn. Fee, User charges, value of
stamp papers etc., in the shape of cash at the Sub-Registrar office for any reason instead of
challan the same shall also be accepted duly recording the éasons.,

9). The Bank will not be accepting Challans from public, which are less than Rs.1000/-

, However, in.case of sale of Special adhesive stamps-instructions were
already issued to sell them through challan only. Therefore, any payments irre-
spective of value towards sale of Special adhesive stamps (In NJ head of account
in the prescribed challan) must be. accepted through challan only, The District
Registrars will co-ordinate with the Banks for accepting the payments in respect
of Special adhesive stamps irrespective of the amounts.

10) All the amounts collected by the Sub-Registrar in cash have to be remitted in to. the designated
branch of the Bank in the prescribed challan at the end of the day / next day,

11) The-Sub-Registrar shall preserve the challans and receipts. produced by:the parties for verifi-
cation in future. Similarly the SRO copies of challans along with scroll sent by the designated
Branch to SRO every day should be preserved.

12) The Recéipt issued by the designated Branch and furnished by the party along with the docu-
‘ments for régistration ‘hag to be Scanned along ‘with the Scannitig’of document, as if it is‘a last
page of the document. While assigning page numbers to the document for scanning purpose

_. two additional page numbers should be added to the Original document for including the scan-
-ning of the receipt. However, the Receipt does not-form part of the document.and should not
be given page endorsement. The above process is only for Scanning purpose. After scanning
“the receipt, it should not be returned to the party and it has to be preserved in m S.R.O, asa
permanent record in a ‘separate file volume., :

13) thé Managementof Banks agreed to. transfet the ‘amount. collected: up.to'the: closure of the

+>, business hours to-Treasury:: _ Branch on ‘the same ‘day by ‘préparing.a current account statement
{Scrol}} and,to transfer the remaining amount collected after the business hours (In the ex-
tended honrs) to. the Treasury. Branch on thenext working day. .;

The Sub-Registrar has to send one of his staff members to the designated Branch on every
working day and see that. the Government challans are prepared Head of account wise(similar
to.the existing procedure) based on the scrvll prepared by the designated Bank and send them
to the Treasury Branch for remittance along with Tr. V/Banker’s cheque/D).D without charg-
ing any. commission issued by the designated Branch. The Sub-Registrar has to sign the Gov-
ernment challans and see that remittances are.made in time. The copies of challans.issued by
the Treasury Branch after remittance should be preserved in the SRO for reconciliation pur-
pose with Treasury.

In the case where the Treasury branch is far off, the existing periodicity of remittances have
to be followed. In every Sub-Registrar office registers in the prescribed proformas should be
opened in the proformas given in Annexure -I in order to facilitate reconciliation with Bank
and Treasury. figures.

(a) Daily Challan Register; In this Register the Sub-Registrar has to note the particulars
of all the challans/Receipts received bv him from the parties.

(b) Pending Challan Register: In this register the Sub-Registrar has to note the details
ofthe challans received from the designated Bank at the end of the day, but not
«presented by the party in the S.R.O. on the date of payment. If the party presents such
challan at a later date, the Sub-Registrar will make'a cross reference entry in pending
challan register as well as in the daily challan register.

The designated branch will send Bank scroll with names of the parties along with challans
(S.R.0 copies) to the Sub-Registrars at the end of the day for verification.

The Sub-Registrars have to ensure that the Bank Scrolls are tallied with thé receipts produced
by the parties every day and that the Bank transfers the amounts to the Treasury Branch as
mentioned: above. These are to. be verified and reconciled every week.-A certificate to this
effect may be‘added in the Challan Registers.

District Registrars concerned may contact the Liasion Officer/District coordinator of the Bank
who is nominated by the Bank for co-ordination and make necessary arrangements.to see that
the Bank itself transfers the T.D. in respect of Municipal Corporations. In respect of other
local Bodies, the existing procedure shall continue to be followed.

It is to be noted that while filling up of the Bank challan form the column relating to 5% TD and

~ 95% TD are applicable to the properties situated in Municipal Corporations only aid other

20)

local bodies (Gram panchayats etc.,) In respect of Municipalities and other local bodies, the
entire Transfer duty has to be paid under the head ‘Deficit stamp duty’ in the challan. The
allocation of Transfer duty to the local bodies and to the department has to be done at the end
of every quarter as. per the existing procedure.

(A) The payment particulars of the Stamp Duty, T.D., Registation fee etc., paid through
challan in the designated Branch of the Bank and Deficit Stamp duty etc., paid in cash at Sub-
Registrar office should be noted separately in the cash receipt, till the provisions are made in
the CARD softwares and pre-printed stationery.

(B) In Account “A” maintained in $.R.O.s, the amounts collected through challan
should be entered in red ink with the particulars of challan and particulars of amount of cash
collected by S.R. in blue / black ink.

(C) In account “H” the un-remitted cash received by the Sub-Registrar at the end
of the day must be shown in blue/black ink and the amounts paid in Bank through challan must
be shown in red ink.

All Sub-Registrars and District Registrars shall be in continuous touch with the authorities of
the respective designated Bank Branches to avoid any operational problems and also complete
the reconciliation work with Treasury by 5" of succeeding month.

In case of payment of cash through challan by a party to a wrong head of account or toa Bank |
branch other than the designated branch of the concerned S.R.O., the remedial orders for
correction of such mistakes may be issued by the District Registrar to the designated branch
before remittance of the amounts to the Treasury by issuing a letter to the designated Branci:
and if such amounts ate transferred into Treasury by issuing an Alteration Memo by the
District Registrar concerned to the Treasury as per the existing procedure.

The ADR of each district has to verify the payment of challans in respect of the documents
registered in each $.R.O and reconciliation of payments with Treasury during the internal
audit. Ail inspecting officers also have to verify the same at random of 5% of the documents
registered whenever they visit the S.R.Os. The Deputy Inspector General concered shall
review this item of work with every Distrcit Registrar and Assistant District Registrar every
month and ensure that the instructions are implemented. A copy of the review made by the
DIG should be submitted to the Inspector General of Registration & Stamps by 5" of Suc-
ceeding month without fail.

The District Registrars and the Asst.District Registrars shall conduct a meeting of all Sub-
Registrars and explain to them in detail the procedure of the new system of payment through
Bank. They have to co-ordinate the process under new system and ensure that the above
instructions are implemented strictly, smoothly and effectively without any scope for com-
plaints.
